# SnipAi QA ledger

**Scope: the SnipAi app** — `app/`, `lib/`, `native/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`,
root config. The `ugc-edit-system/` pipeline is out of scope; findings already
made there are kept at the bottom for reference and are not worked on unless
asked (`./scripts/qa --with-pipeline` re-enables its checks).

Every finding QA has confirmed, and what happened to it. This file is the
memory between runs — `/qa` reads it before reporting so it tells you what is
*new* instead of repeating itself, and updates it after.

**Status:** `open` · `fixed` (with date) · `wontfix` (a decision, not an
oversight) · `regressed` (was fixed, came back).
**Test:** the regression test that reproduces it. A test here that starts
passing means the bug is fixed — that is the signal to flip the row.

Run `./scripts/qa --regressions` to see the bug board. Run `/qa-fix S1 S2` to
work through them.

**How rows are counted — one definition, settled 2026-09-10.** Two different
totals were circulating, which is how a ledger stops being trusted. The number
anyone quotes is the **finish-line count**: live rows (`open` + `regressed`,
excluding `fixed`, `wontfix` and `retired`) in **Server, Client, Native and
shell, Tooling, and the two Edit-quality sections** — the sections that stand
between the app and shipping. **Unused (U), Half-built (H), Bloat (B) and Repo
hygiene (G) are excluded**: each is its own milestone (M3, M4, M5) or its own
role's checklist, and folding them in inflates the number that is supposed to
mean "defects left". Pipeline (P) is quoted separately, never folded in. The
earlier "69" was every live row in the file; it was not wrong arithmetic, it
was the wrong denominator, and the finish-line count is the one that governs. **One more number exists and is not a rival:** `guard-ledger.py` prints *"N row(s) marked open or regressed have no test on the board"* (93 today) and labels it, in its own output and in a comment, as **not** the defect count — it counts every live row in the file, U/H/B/G and pipeline included, and only says which have no board test. Dev added that disclaimer deliberately so a script could not undo this rule. Quote the finish-line count; quote 93 only as coverage.

**Who flips a row, and when — settled 2026-09-11 after I got it wrong.** Two
different acts by two different roles, and conflating them produced a paradox
that looked like a contradiction in the rules. **`snipai-dev` flips the row in
the same commit as the code and the test** — PROCESS.md's Definition of Done
items 4 and 5 require exactly that, and `guard-ledger` depends on it: the guard
reddens the moment a board test passes while its row still says `open`, so a
fix branch is red by construction until the flip lands with it. **The PM flips
a row only on observed evidence** — a merge plus a suite result, or a tester
report. Never from a diff, a working tree, or a file read off disk. On
2026-09-11 I marked C22, C19 and C20 `fixed` from an uncommitted tree that no
one had run; the fix turned out to be real and the claim was still unfounded,
and it went into the one document the gates are checked against. Reverted, then
flipped again on `e761337` with 13 green tests observed. The rule is not "wait
for dev" — dev's atomic commit is correct and required. The rule is that
**anything *I* write about a fix has to come from a run somebody saw.**

**Rows are not milestones — settled 2026-09-11, the second definitional rule
this file has needed and from the same confusion as the first.** A row tracks
a **code defect**: it is `fixed` when the code is fixed and its test is green.
A milestone tracks a **verified outcome**: it closes when the role that can
verify it says so. "Merged but not shipped" is a state a row has never been
able to express and should not have to — it belongs in the milestone's exit
line. If an artifact check later shows the fix did not reach the app, the row
goes **`regressed`**, which is exactly what that status is for.

Both halves of the confusion bit within one session. **M0.6 was closed on a
merge** — a milestone taking a row's word for a verified outcome. Then
`./scripts/bundle-app` **deadlocked**: `guard-ledger` blocked the rebundle
because N1's row was open, and N1's row was open pending the rebundle — a row
being asked to carry an artifact's status. Opposite directions, one error. The
guard's contract, "test green ↔ row fixed", was sound throughout and was not
weakened to resolve it.

**THE POSTABLE QUESTION — named 2026-09-11 after Kayer said what a finished
video is, and it re-weights rows rather than adding one.** His words: *"I want
this app bulletproof solid and these videos to be super tightly cut. my usual
time i have a finished video is about a minute."*

**Every quality measure this project has built asks "is this cut correct?"
Not one of them asks "is this a video he would post?"** The scorecard,
`verify_edges.py`, `verify_removals.py`, `verify_cut.py`, the repeats metric,
the word-cutoff metric — all of them check the cut against the edit. None
checks the cut against what he wants. **A cut can pass every one of them and be
three minutes long, which is exactly what happened tonight.**

**The evidence was in his library the whole time, and it is the part worth being
uncomfortable about.** `img-9817` has **six** rendered versions and `img-9823`
has two:

| file | length | built |
|---|---|---|
| `img-9817-v1.mp4` | 1:50.36 | Sep 7 17:05 |
| `img-9817-v2.mp4` | 1:47.96 | Sep 8 15:34 |
| `img-9817-v3.mp4` | 1:51.69 | Sep 8 19:40 |
| `img-9817-v4.mp4` | 1:51.02 | Sep 8 22:05 |
| `img-9817-v5.mp4` | 1:38.49 | Sep 9 **00:15** |
| `img-9817-v6.mp4` | 1:38.39 | Sep 9 **07:07** |
| `img-9823-v1.mp4` | 3:04.29 | |
| `img-9823-v2.mp4` | 2:58.92 | |

**Six builds over 38 hours, each a little shorter than the last, one at
quarter past midnight and the next at seven in the morning — and he never got
below 1:38 against a target of about 1:00. Then he stopped.** The other project
he abandoned at 2:59. **That is a person fighting the tool by hand, losing, and
giving up 38 seconds over the line.** We have cited `img-9817` all day as a
convenient real project to cross-check numbers against, and **nobody asked why
there were six of them.** The version history was a bug report written in the
file system, available since before any of this started.

**AND NOW THERE IS A REFERENCE SET: eight videos he cut himself, 2026-09-11.**
Same instrument throughout, `silencedetect=n=-35dB:d=0.25`:

**CORRECTED TO SEVEN 2026-09-11: Kayer withdrew `5ca5169e` himself** — *"deliberate
pause for effect. I was gargling mouthwash and sped it up."* and *"You won't get a
lot of those. I should not have included that as an example."* **Withdrawn by his
instruction, not by our statistics**, which matters: we kept it in deliberately
rather than averaging it out, and he removed it as a bad example.

| file | duration | silence | cuts/min | avg shot |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ef69d650` | 37.4s | **0.00s** | 28.9 | 1.97s |
| `f4f5b159` | 44.1s | **0.00s** | 21.7 | 2.60s |
| `d4a94f7c` | 45.9s | 0.44s (tail) | 17.0 | 3.28s |
| `443f5cff` | 54.5s | **0.00s** | 27.5 | 2.09s |
| `b08bd9bf` | 57.0s | **0.00s** | 23.2 | 2.48s |
| `36c80188` | 57.5s | 0.54s (tail) | 25.1 | 2.30s |
| `bbe0c896` | 83.4s | **0.00s** | 25.2 | 2.32s |

| app output | duration | silence | cuts/min | avg shot |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-clip | 190.1s | **6.2%** | 28.1 | 2.11s |
| 2-clip | 105.0s | **5.6%** | 27.4 | 2.14s |
| `img-9817` v6 | 98.4s | 1.4% | 22.6 | 2.59s |

**THE GAP ON THE CORRECTED SET: rhythm matched, silence off by five to six
times, duration off by three.** Rhythm measured by scene-change detection at
threshold 0.15, detections within 0.3s collapsed; script re-runnable at
`scratchpad/rhythm.sh`. **Method caveat every citing row must carry: scene
detection finds cuts by picture discontinuity, so it misses a cut where he
barely moved between takes. Every count is a FLOOR, not an exact figure** — and
the effect is symmetric across his videos and the app's, so the comparison holds
even where the absolute numbers are low.

**AND THIS IS THE FOURTH TIME A PROXY HAS BEEN SATISFIED WHILE THE PROPERTY
FAILED — this time in our own analysis rather than in the product.** Kayer
retargeted the work: *"Forget the rule of my videos being about a minute the
important thing is how fast the cuts are."* **Measured, the app already cuts at
his rhythm** — 28.1 cuts/min against his 17.0-28.9, a 2.11s average shot against
his 1.97-3.28s, **all three app outputs inside his range on both measures, and
tonight's cut faster than six of his seven videos.** So on the axis he named,
there is no gap. **But cuts-per-minute is a PROXY for "how fast the cuts are",
and the app satisfies the proxy while failing the property**, for two reasons
that are both already filed: **a cut that lands and then waits** (E7 — 5.6-6.2%
silence and a shipped 1.99s internal gap against his zero) and **a cut that
lands and repeats the line it just said** (E5 — eight attempts at one line
across 24 of 33.75s). **Both feel slow to a viewer while the cut counter reads
correct.** `verify_edges.py` certified a clipping build, a string assertion
would certify P6's dead `drawbox`, `verify_cut.py`'s FAIL reached no surface —
and now a rhythm metric built from his own videos certifies a 190-second cut he
would not post.

**What follows for the metrics, and it is a reframe rather than a demotion:**
**cuts/min is retired as a target and kept as a REGRESSION GUARD.** It is
already right, so its remaining job is to stop a fix for E5 or E7 breaking a
rhythm that matches his. **A matched metric's value is preventing regression,
not driving work.**

**`5ca5169e`'s withdrawal barely moved rhythm at all, and that is the
interesting part:** it sat at **24.9 cuts/min, dead centre** of his range, while
being a 15% outlier on silence — because he sped up a long non-speech stretch,
so the cuts stayed normal and the gap did not. **Second independent reason to
treat rhythm as the robust signal.**

Against what the app produces: 3-clip **190.09s / 25 gaps / 11.78s / 6.2%**;
2-clip **105.02s / 10 / 5.90s / 5.6%**; `img-9817` v6 **98.39s / 4 / 1.38s /
1.4%**; `img-9823` v2 **178.92s / 14 / 4.65s / 2.6%**.

**(a) Dead air is an absolute with NO exceptions — filed as E7, and the withdrawal made this clean rather than seven-of-eight.** **Five of seven contain exactly 0.00s** of detectable pause; the other two have a single gap each, **0.44s and 0.54s, both at the tail.** So *no internal dead air* is not a tendency with an outlier attached — **it is a rule he does not break.** The pipeline's ~3s flag threshold and tonight's shipped **1.99s** internal gap **permit a thing that never occurs in his work.**

**(b) Air and length are independent problems, and his own hand work proves the
first is reachable without the second.** `img-9817` v6 reached **1.4% silence —
his own standard, by hand, over six versions — and is still 98.39s** against a
median reference of ~50s. **Strip every millisecond of air out of v6 and it is
97 seconds.** The most useful single measurement in the set.

**(c) But the app is NOT cutting timidly — it already cuts harder than he does,
and the length gap is upstream of the cut.** His own `reference/house-style.json`
records his reference pair as **332.8s raw → 67.47s finished = 20.3% kept**.
Tonight: 3-clip **190.09 of 1130.63 = 16.8%**; 2-clip **105.02 of 548.80 =
19.1%**. **The app keeps a smaller fraction of his footage than he did by hand.**
So "most of the reduction must come from leaving content out" is arithmetically
true for reaching 50s and **misdiagnoses where the slack is**: the output is long
because **he recorded 18:50 of source for a video that should run about a
minute.** That is E5 from the other end, and **the lever is collapsing
near-duplicate beats, not deciding which of his lines are cut.**

**The outlier gets a question, not an average.** `5ca5169e` is 40.97s with
**6.14s of silence in three gaps, 15%** — an order of magnitude from the other
seven, in his shortest-but-one video. Music or b-roll under a silent stretch, a
deliberate beat, or a different format; he has been asked. **Seven-of-eight is
the pattern and that file is unexplained rather than contradictory.** And it
does not need solving separately: **the app's own reference machinery already
takes the MEDIAN** — `app/api/references/route.ts:167`, *"measure_finished.py
across reference/inspiration — median of them all"* — which is outlier-resistant
by construction.

**Caveat every citing row must carry: these evidence EDITING DECISIONS ONLY.**
All eight are 576x1024 TikTok-served copies, and `36c80188` is a `.mov`
re-export of a served copy rather than an upload master. **No artifact we have
carries his export settings**, so none of this bears on M0.95, whose
full-resolution target has no reference to aim at except *matches the source*.

**Where they live is itself a problem: eight files, 2.4-4.3 MB, ~25 MB total, in
`~/.claude/uploads/7bd12c30-.../`** — a chat upload directory. Not the repo, not
his library, **nowhere a build or a test can reach**, for the only examples in
existence of the output this app is meant to produce. **Which is why the numbers
above are in this file and not only in a message:** the measurements are the
durable part; the artifacts matter for re-measuring. DOCKET has where they go
and who moves them.

**What this changes here, concretely:** **E5** was downgraded to Low tonight and
that was assessed against the wrong yardstick — see its row. **S26** stops being
a display defect and becomes the wrong instrument for the only judgement he is
trying to make. **And no new metric closes this**, because a metric is what let
six versions of a too-long video read as a working feature. The exit condition
for **M0.85** is his sentence, verified by him.

| T6 | `./scripts/qa --full` resolved `DATA_ROOT` to the real library and evicted his whole job history into `_test_*` fixtures | fixed 2026-09-11 (`02d1225`) — **M0.6** |

| C32 | `review/page.tsx:1192` — **Trash says "Sent back to re-cut" and bins the video instead**, with no way back to `unreviewed` from any button | fixed 2026-09-11 — **wrong sentence, defensible action**; Trash deletes nothing (measured), and "bins" was the row overstating it |
| S25 | `lib/pipeline.ts:296` — "Plan graphics" a second time overwrites `work/graphics.json` whole: every hand-added graphic, every edited definition, every `verified` flag gone | open |
| S34 | `lib/stitch.ts:139-142` — the edit-list guard refuses a join on **every clip Kayer owns**; multi-clip import is 0% functional on his own footage | fixed 2026-09-11, **and verified by `snipai-tester` in the packaged app: two of his real clips joined with `blocker: null` into a 1,635,126,156-byte 9:08.80 source and built into one cut.** First time this feature has worked on his footage **Corrected same day:** the guard failed OPEN on a probe it could not measure (`undefined` passes a `=== null` check, the arithmetic yields NaN, and every comparison against NaN is false), so it waved through the one case it exists to refuse. Unmeasurable now means refuse, not pass. | fixed 2026-09-11 — decides from the **video** track's edit list, where a trim shows and priming does not; all eight of his clips hide 0.0000s of picture |
| S35 | `lib/stitch.ts:266-267` — the drift tolerance is tighter than the drift a correct join produces; a good two-clip join is refused for 0.1s | **open — premise not reproduced on three independent attempts, original fixture lost, no further reproduction planned.** Window hardened in the S34 commit. Measured drift: Theo ~0.04s, PM ~0.04s, and now **a real two-clip join in the packaged app — inputs summing 548.76s against a joined 548.80s, 0.04s against a 0.067s window.** The reported 0.1s has never appeared. The `clipA`/`clipB` recipe is **not recoverable** — the preserved corpus kept the outputs and not the ffmpeg commands — and the tester declined to guess at it, which is right. **Left open rather than closed `wontfix`:** three non-reproductions of a reported number is grounds to stop looking, not grounds to assert the number was wrong. If it reappears it arrives with a recipe. **And this window carried the IDENTICAL fail-open, one check along — found in `bb345e7` and not spotted when the first one was flagged.** The same `NaN` reached here: `predictedReturn` summed it, `tolerance` became `NaN`, and `Math.abs(drift) > NaN` is **`false`**. **So two guards in the same path both certified as safe a clip nobody could read**, which is a better argument for N18 than any count of type errors. **One deliberate asymmetry, recorded so nobody "corrects" it later:** `predictedReturn` treats `null` as **0** on purpose, because that keeps the drift window **tighter** rather than widening it by a number nobody has. `joinRefusal` refuses on `null`; this one tightens on it. Same input, opposite handling, both fail-closed |
| S36 | `lib/stitch.ts:268-274` — a refused join leaves its **546 MB** joined file in `raw/`, and the project name it failed under is then taken | fixed 2026-09-11 — both halves asserted red-then-green, name reusability included. **Held under test in the packaged app:** staging swept the instant the join landed, free space 1614 MB -> 2137 MB at 14:55, `.staging/` empty, the project name never stranded |
| S42 | the automatic post-edit re-export — deleting **one line** turned a 20 MB 406x720 cut into a **505 MB 2160x3840** one and grew `work/clips/` from 19.6 MiB to **768 MiB**; ~25x the disk for the most ordinary action in the app | fixed 2026-09-11 — an automatic re-export now keeps the resolution of the cut on disk, and `work/clips` is emptied at the start of every build. **Correction: the disk was 96% resolution, 4% orphaning** |
| C1 | `Timeline.tsx:298` — the in-point drag runs away and collapses the clip | fixed 2026-09-08 (`bb4bd5f`) |
| C2 | `review/page.tsx:1276` — a second trim started before the first PATCH returns gets no history entry | open — partial, the once-per-page-load half is fixed |
| T1 | `scripts/test:26` — a skipped suite reads as "all green" | fixed |
| S18 | `media/[...path]/route.ts:73` — 416 on a range ending past EOF; **no video plays anywhere in the app** | fixed |
| S19 | the step writing `cuts/*.mp4` — `moov` at end of file, so the export can never start streaming | fixed |
| E1 | beat out-points clip the final consonant of two lines (`under-eyes-looks`, `egf-going-improve`) | fixed 2026-09-10 on dev (26 clips, 0 short); packaged pending the rebundle — see T5 |

**Why S34/S35 are in this table when neither corrupts a file.** They are the
open milestone's own blocker and they fail on 100% of his footage: M0.8 exists
because he never shoots a single clip, and today the join refuses every
multi-clip import he could make. S36 does lose something — 546 MB per failed
import on a volume that has been at 98%.

Out of scope but still true: `snap_tail` was **reset to 0.01 on 2026-09-10**
(`~/Movies/SnipAi/state/tuning.json`, `overrides_seen: 0`), so P2's 0.881
poisoning is no longer live. P1's missing clamp is still unfixed, so nothing
stops it recurring the next time learning runs.

## Server — `app/api/**`, `lib/`

| ID | Where | Defect | Status | Test |

| S1 | `pipeline/route.ts:136` | Beat rebuilt from a 4-field whitelist; undo drops `holes`, `audioStart`/`audioEnd`, `fadeIn`/`fadeOut` | **wontfix 2026-09-09** — merging from disk on undo would make every cut permanent. The live loss needs GET to drop a field; it does not (6 fields pinned round-tripping in `tests/undo-round-trip.test.mts`) | `tests/regressions/S1-*.test.mts` (kept red on purpose) |
| S2 | `lib/jobs.ts:184` | `failJob` is the one mutator that doesn't `persist()`; failed builds stay "running" everywhere else and then 409 the next build | fixed — `failJob` calls `persist()`; its regression test is green | `tests/regressions/S2-*.test.mts` |
| S3 | `projects/route.ts:68` | Upload buffered whole, twice, no size cap; a 1.4GB original OOMs and the catch then deletes the new project dir. **Stale** — commit `7d31529` ("Stop the import taking the machine down") replaced the real upload path with a 64KB-chunk stream; `DropZone.tsx` sends the raw/streamed request, not multipart. The bug still exists at `references/route.ts:60`, which is a separate, lower-traffic route | fixed — confirmed 2026-09-11 for the footage-import path; `references/route.ts:60` still open | no — needs a large-file harness |
| S4 | `lib/pipeline.ts:183-184`, `lib/jobs.ts:197` and `:201-204` | Unbounded stdout accumulation over a 6-hour cap, and unbounded `job.log` re-serialised every 2s. Defect unchanged; line numbers corrected 2026-09-10 (`:149`/`:164` were stale) | open | no |
| S5 | `peaks/route.ts:56`, `beats/[label]/candidates/route.ts:40` | CWD-relative `projects/<name>` where every other caller passes an absolute path; take picker 422s after migration. **Already fixed and the row was stale for a day** — commit `8289149` ("Four tools were being handed a path that has not existed since the migration") fixed all four routes at once; both halves now pass `projectDir(project)` | fixed 2026-09-09 (`8289149`) | no — covered by the commit's own change |
| S6 | `lib/pipeline.ts:32` | `spawnSync` + `import faster_whisper` on the request path of eight GETs, 60s cache | open | no |
| S7 | `beats/route.ts:87` | Split spreads holes and detached audio onto both halves; the line renders twice | **fixed 2026-09-09** — `lib/splitBeat.ts` divides instead of copying | `tests/regressions/S7-*.test.mts` (now green) + `tests/undo-round-trip.test.mts` |
| S8 | `beats/[label]/route.ts:166` → `lib/beats.ts:88-90` | **A trimmed line can vanish from the finished video without saying so.** `updateBeatRange` sets `beat.start`/`beat.end` directly and never re-runs `normaliseHoles`, and the PATCH path validates only `end - start >= 0.15`. Beat 10.0-20.0 with hole `[12.0,18.0]`, trimmed to `{start:12.5, end:15.0}`, now has a hole covering the whole beat: `walk_pieces` emits nothing, so the line is absent from the cut while still sitting in `beats.json` looking fine, and `layout()` draws it 0px wide so it cannot be clicked to find out why. Cited `:136` was stale — that line is now a `catch`. **Promoted to Fix first 2026-09-10 and scheduled as M0.75** (see DOCKET); measured against NOTES.md's "drop in raw footage and it edits my TikToks", a line disappearing from the cut silently is close to the worst thing the app can do | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `S8-a-trim-cannot-swallow-a-line.test.mts`. **Where the invariant went, and why there rather than at the reported line:** into `updateBeatRange` (`lib/beats.ts`), which is the *only* place a beat's picture edges are written anywhere in the app. The reported caller was the trim PATCH; the other caller is the take picker (`candidates/route.ts:92`), which moves the edges further than any drag does and would have been the second door to the same harm. Putting the check in the route would have left a permissive `updateBeatRange` for the next caller to find — the same argument as the count check inside `ourListeningPIDs` and the ratio off the box in C30: **fix the class, not the instance.** The function's return type is now a union, so tsc — not review — is what stops a third call site reading `.changed` without having handled the refusal. The rule itself lives in `lib/holes.ts:keptAfterTrim`, beside `normaliseHoles`: the same question asked in the other direction (that one holds the edges still and moves the holes; this one holds the holes still and moves the edges, and only one of the two was ever checked). **What it does NOT do is rewrite the holes** — `build_cut` clamps them to the beat as it reads them, and persisting the clamped version would throw a hole's real edges away the moment a line was trimmed in and back out, quietly resurrecting footage deleted on purpose. **What the app does now: refuses, and says so.** Not clamp (there is no nearby valid range — everything between those edges is holed, so any clamp is a guess at what the drag meant) and not drop-the-hole (that silently resurrects a deliberate cut, which is the same class of harm pointed the other way). 400 + a sentence: *"everything between those edges is already cut out of this line — put back the cut inside it first, or delete the line"*, or for the near-miss case the exact number of seconds that would have been left. **The surface was the other half of the fix, and without it this row would have traded one silent failure for another:** `review/page.tsx:1320`'s timeline-drag handler was `else { await load(); }` — a reload that snaps the handle back and is indistinguishable from a drag that did not take. That is ledger BUG-011 ("delete says it cut, and cuts nothing") re-achieved through a new door, so it now toasts the server's sentence. `saveTrim` already did. **Proof of both halves, because a TypeScript-only test would have gone green all night while the line still vanished:** 9 tests, 4 of which drive `walk_pieces` in `build_cut.py` directly (pure arithmetic — no video, no ffmpeg, nothing written). The first asserts the *harm* still reproduces before asserting the guard, so the file cannot quietly become vacuous. The last two are a 775-case sweep of start × end over a holed beat, cross-checked against `walk_pieces` case by case: **0 accepted-and-renders-nothing, 0 refused-but-would-have-rendered — 476 accepted all render, 299 refused all render zero pieces**, an exact agreement rather than a safe over-approximation. Against the old `end - start >= 0.15` rule the same sweep has **292 of 775** accepted trims rendering as nothing, so the sweep is a real test and not the shape that passes either way. One more test pins that holes are the only route to an empty beat — a detected pause cannot empty one, since the head guard skips a pause within 0.18s of the in-point and a tail-reaching pause only moves the out-point — which is what makes a hole-shaped guard the right shape | **fixed 2026-09-11** | yes — `tests/regressions/S8-a-trim-cannot-swallow-a-line.test.mts` (9 tests) |

| S34 | `lib/stitch.ts:139-142` (`joinRefusal`), calibrated at `lib/clipProbe.ts:44` | **The guard that refuses trimmed clips fires on untrimmed ones, so no join of Kayer's footage can ever succeed — multi-clip import is 0% functional on every clip he owns.** `joinRefusal` refuses when `editListTrimSec(p) > 1 / fps` — **0.0333s at 30fps**. Measured on Nadia's six new 4K clips via `~/Movies/SnipAi/qa-m08/qa-transcripts/probes.json`, `rawDurationSec - durationSec` is **0.05, 0.06, 0.05, 0.05, 0.09, 0.05**, and both files in his real library measure **0.0600**. That delta is AAC encoder priming, not a trim, so **every clip he owns exceeds the threshold**. The refusal sentence is false in both halves: *"IMG_0060.MOV was trimmed after it was filmed — Photos does that without re-encoding"* (nothing was trimmed) and *"Duplicate it in Photos and import the copy"* (**the remedy cannot work** — a Photos duplicate carries the same priming offset). **COPY family, and the third member that is actively wrong rather than merely unhelpful** — and the worst kind, because he will act on it: duplicate a 4K clip in Photos, spend the disk, and be refused again. **The file documents its own contradiction:** `clipProbe.ts:44` reads *"(measured on IMG_9817: 0.044s, which is just AAC priming)"* — **0.044 exceeds the 0.0333 threshold the same module's guard sets.** The guard was calibrated against footage it also rejects and says so in a comment. **Widening the frame count is the wrong fix, and Nadia said so explicitly:** `stitch.ts:134-137` records what a *real* edit-list trim measures — *"two 4s chunks whose edit lists hid 1.1s each joined to 9.34s instead of 8.34s"* — and **1.1s against 0.05s is a 20x separation**, so the two cases are genuinely distinguishable. The fix is to read the `elst` atom rather than infer a trim from a duration delta | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `S34-the-join-guard-measures-the-picture.test.mts` (7 tests). **The exit condition is met, including the "decides from the `elst` rather than from `rawDurationSec - durationSec`" clause — but not the way the row expected, and the difference is the fix.** `ffprobe` does **not exist on this machine** (`clipProbe.ts` says so itself: the venv ships the ffmpeg binary alone), so `-show_entries` was not available. What works with ffmpeg alone is to demux **one track at a time** to `-f null` with and without `-ignore_editlist` and difference each track against itself. Measured 2026-09-11 on all eight real files: **video identical both ways on every one of them — 0.0000s of picture hidden** (IMG_0062: video 215.24/215.24, audio 215.31→215.36). The same clip after `ffmpeg -ss 1.1 -c copy`: **video 4.83→6.00**. So a trim hides *picture* and priming does not, and the two cases separate by mechanism rather than by magnitude — his clips hide exactly nothing, not "a little". `videoEditListTrimSec` is the new instrument; `editListTrimSec` remains only as a documented fallback when the per-track measurement is missing, because absent is not zero. **The one allowance left is derived, not picked:** B-frame reorder delay is a genuine video edit list on re-encoded material (measured 0.07s on x264 defaults) and is bounded by reorder depth, so the fixture set has both a no-B-frame clip shaped like his camera and a B-frame clip, and a guard that passes only one is calibrated to an encoder. **Explicitly not a widened threshold** — a 0.2s Photos trim would pass any constant set clear of his 0.09s and is refused here. **The message stopped diagnosing:** no Photos, no "duplicate it", because we can measure that picture is hidden and cannot measure what hid it — same family as C35 and C36. **Cost:** one extra pass over each clip's video packets at probe time (seconds on a 612MB 4K clip), next to a Whisper transcription of the same clip that costs minutes. **Non-vacuity is asserted first:** test 1 proves the old threshold really did refuse all eight, so the file cannot quietly become meaningless **One judgement call, flagged because it went against the row and an existing test caught it.** The first draft removed the Photos remedy outright, on the row's reasoning that it "cannot work". `tests/stitch.test.mts`'s "a clip trimmed without re-encoding is refused by name" then went red — it requires *"Duplicate it in Photos"*, the hidden amount, two remedies, and no jargon. **That test was not edited to agree with the fix.** Read against the new instrument it is right and the row is half wrong: the remedy cannot work on an UNTRIMMED clip (which is all S34 was about, and which no longer reaches this branch), but on a clip that measurably hides picture a Photos duplicate is re-encoded and does bake the trim in. So the sentence now offers it conditionally — *"Duplicate it in Photos and import the copy if that is where the trim came from, or import X on its own"* — which states no cause as fact, keeps a remedy that works whatever the cause, and satisfies both tests. **Second opinion taken and acted on 2026-09-11 (follow-up commit, not an amend -- `2bb2b96` is in Ruth's shipped bundle).** The wording was reviewed as too dense: it led with a comparison to an abstraction (*"more hidden inside it than a recording carries of its own accord"*) and trailed the condition as *"if that is where the trim came from"*. Both fair. The proposed replacement was **run against `tests/stitch.test.mts` rather than accepted**, and the test refused it -- it misses `/1\.\d\ds of it is still in the file/` and `/Duplicate it in Photos/`. **The test won again and was not bent, including when the suggestion came from the reviewer.** The shipped sentence is a third form that takes the readability win and passes all five checks: *"X was shortened after filming: 1.50s of it is still in the file, just hidden. Joining brings that back and pushes everything after it late. Duplicate it in Photos and import the copy if you trimmed it there. Otherwise import X on its own."* -- **44 words against 67**, the condition moved to where he can answer it, no cause asserted. **A finding worth its own row came out of this and is NOT fixed here:** those two assertions are over-specified against their own comments -- the reviewer's sentence *does* state how much is hidden and *does* offer the Photos remedy, and was refused on phrasing rather than on the property the comment names. Same class as C22/C32/C35: asserting the instruction instead of the output. My own `/if that is where/` assertion had the identical fault and was rewritten to `/Duplicate it in Photos[^.]*\bif\b/`, the property rather than the words  **The ruling that brought it here:** **Critical. The next row, paired with S35 and S36 in one commit** **CORRECTION 2026-09-11, same day: this mechanism had a hole and now does not.** The guard **failed OPEN**. `editListTrimSec` and `videoEditListTrimSec` tested `=== null`, but a `ClipProbe` built by a caller that predates S42's per-track fields carries **`undefined`** there — which passes a null check, so the subtraction ran on nothing and produced **`NaN`**. Every comparison against NaN is false, so `NaN > allowance` was false and `joinRefusal` answered **"no blocker" for a clip it could not measure at all.** The one case this row exists to catch was the one case it waved through, while the ledger said the danger was handled. The same NaN also reached S35's window (`predictedReturn`), making `tolerance` NaN and the drift check false — the identical fail-open one check along. **Both now answer `null` for "not measurable", which is not 0:** zero is a measurement saying nothing is hidden, null says nobody knows, and `joinRefusal` **refuses** when neither read is available rather than certifying. `predictedReturn` treats null as 0 deliberately, because that keeps the drift window tighter rather than widening it by a number nobody has. **Fail-closed is not fail-always** — a test pins that his own eight clips still pass on the container fallback when the per-track read is missing. **Found sideways, from S38's test, and the root cause is its own row:** no test file has ever been typechecked, so `tests/grouping.test.mts`'s `probe()` has been building an incomplete `ClipProbe` unnoticed. The orphans are the ORIGINAL 720p build and they are **3.9%** of the directory; **the other 96% is the resolution defect.** Both are real and both are fixed, but anyone budgeting disk should know the orphaning was never the expensive half. **Part 1, and the rule is deliberately narrower than the row implies.** `review/page.tsx`'s auto-apply posted a body identical to the Build button's, so the server could not tell them apart and always took `:575`. It now sends `auto: true`, and `runBuildJob` renders at whatever the cut already on disk is. Stated as an invariant: **an action he did not ask for does not change the resolution, in either direction** — so an automatic re-export after an explicit full-res Build stays full res rather than quietly downgrading his picture, which is asserted too. **This does not decide Part 5 #9** (whether a one-click import should end in a full-resolution render); that stays Kayer's. **It does make the answer look more consequential, which is worth saying rather than encoding:** whichever route the first build takes is now the route every later automatic re-export takes, so the import's choice propagates for the life of the project. **The instrument compares the cut against its SOURCE, not against 720**, because a fixed threshold calls a full-res render of 480p footage a proxy; it returns `null` rather than guessing when either probe fails, and absent is not treated as "full". Documented edge: on a source under 720 short-side, `scale=-2:720` upscales and the routes stop being distinguishable. **Parts 2 and 3.** `work/clips` is emptied at the START of every build, which makes the derived-key orphaning impossible rather than cleaned up — there is no surviving generation for a shifted index to land beside, and a post-hoc sweep would have to re-guess which files the new EDL references, which is the same guess that caused it. The pieces are also removed after a successful concat, on the success path only, so a failed build stays diagnosable. **Mara's scope check confirmed independently rather than inherited:** `work/clips` is written at `build_cut.py:360`/`:527` and read by nothing in `lib/`, `app/` or `tools/` — `importBatch.ts:94` 's "clips" is the `.staging` directory, a different thing — and no step's `done()` tests it (`:427` tests `cuts/`, `:426` the proxy). So emptying it cannot make a step re-run. **Test notes worth keeping.** The start-of-build clear needed its own test to mean anything: the end-of-build removal collects orphans too, so every other assertion passes with the start clear deleted. It is isolated by planting a stale generation, failing the build after the clear, and asserting the stale files are gone. The first build goes through the real `runPipelinePhases` rather than an emulation — an earlier draft used `runBuildJob` for it and compared full against full, which passed and proved nothing. And `lib/paths.ts` caching `SNIPAI_DATA` at import bit again (C32's trap): five per-test libraries silently became one, cut versions climbed to v3. **Cost to declare: this file adds 60-130s to the board**, nearly all of it `verify_cut.py` loading a Whisper model it does not need for these claims (11.5s on a 3-second clip). It is down to the two real builds the exit condition names; everything else is asserted without one. If that is too slow for a board that runs constantly, the honest fix is a way to build without scoring, and that is a row rather than something to bodge here  **The ruling that brought it here:** **High, and the next row.** Fix-first not on abstract severity but because it is **the failure that takes his machine down and it fires on the most ordinary action in the app** — two or three edits from out-of-space on a startup disk that has been at 98%, where `ENOSPC` presents as a dropped connection, a confusion this ledger already records. It also unblocks the three-clip run S37's exit needs, and collapses the 37-minute build | yes, not yet written. **Exit: an automatic post-edit re-export produces a proxy-resolution cut, and `work/clips/` after the edit holds no piece file that the current EDL does not reference.** **Scope widened by PM ruling 2026-09-11 and verified before ruling, not assumed: this row also removes the build's scratch when the build ends.** Nothing in `lib/`, `app/` or `tools/` reads `work/clips/` — a grep across all three returns no reader — and **no step's `done()` check tests it** (`lib/pipeline.ts:427` tests `cuts/` for a finished file, `:426` tests the proxy). So deleting it at build end breaks no skip-logic and makes the orphaning impossible rather than merely tidied. It is a few lines inside the file this row already opens, and splitting it into M0.9 would mean two passes over one function. **M0.9 owns the other three locations** (past cut versions, `work/source-proxy.mp4`, `work/strips/`), which are policy rather than a defect. **Confirmed present in the 13:46 bundle 2026-09-11, as expected and not a new defect:** one line deletion took the cut from **38,648,408 bytes / 406x720 to 895,605,766 / 2160x3840 — 23x** — `work/clips/` **not emptied at build start (242 files)**, rebuild ~3x slower. **It capped the three-clip run at one edit and cost the refresh-mid-edit and import-during-build cases outright**, so it is now also a tax on QA coverage and not only on his disk. **And Kayer's proxy decision settles the direction of this fix rather than complicating it** — see M0.95: the automatic path returning to proxy resolution is what he asked for, and full resolution belongs to an export step that does not exist yet |

Two clips sharing a basename are now staged beside each other. **WHICH FIX, and the other option was defensible.** A refusal naming the collision was the alternative. Staging beside won because nothing is lost either way and a refusal sends him to Finder to rename his own footage for something the app can handle without losing a byte — and because his standing preference, *propose, do not decide*, is about judgements over his content, not about where a file lands. **He is told:** the tray row reads *"another clip is already called IMG_0060.MOV, so this one is kept as 2-IMG_0060.MOV"*, and `StagedFile.originalName` keeps what he dropped so the tray can say it later. The exit's primary clause — the count he sees matches the count on disk — is met directly rather than through the fallback. **THE DISAMBIGUATOR IS A PREFIX, and that is not cosmetic.** `lib/stitch.ts`'s `filenameNumber` reads the **trailing** number of a name to order a roll when capture stamps are missing, so `IMG_0060-2.MOV` would read as **2** and sort ahead of `IMG_0060.MOV` — quietly reordering his video to fix a filename clash. `2-IMG_0060.MOV` leaves the trailing number alone, and a test holds that invariant rather than the comment holding it. **Found by checking the ordering fallback before choosing a suffix, not after.** **Three dangerous directions pinned, because the fix could have introduced each.** An upload retried into the same batch stays idempotent — same name AND same size is the same clip, which is what the client's own queue dedupe already assumes — so a retry does not stage a second copy. A file on disk with **no record beside it** is not overwritten either, which is this row's own aftermath and would have been missed by a resolver that consulted only `b.files`. And an ordinary clip is untouched, carrying no extra field for the tray to explain. **Non-vacuity: with the resolver removed, 5 of the 8 go red**, the headline reporting *"two clips were dropped and 1 are on disk"*. The 3 that stay green are the must-not-regress guards, which is the right split. **A test of mine passed for the wrong reason and was caught:** the sanitisation case used two names that do not in fact collapse to the same safe name, so it proved nothing until the collapse itself was asserted. `my clip!.MOV` and `my+clip?.MOV` both reduce to `my_clip_.MOV`; `my-clip@.MOV` does not. **Mara's pairing argument held up from the code:** with S54 in, a dropped clip has somewhere to surface, so this fix had a working failure channel to use that it did not have earlier tonight. **High, and second in the sequence after S54** — S54 is what would make this visible. **Exit: two clips that resolve to the same staged name are refused by name, or kept distinct; neither is silently replaced** **The ruling that brought it here:** open —  | yes, not yet written |
| S62 | `lib/stitch.ts:297` and `:352-367` | **A correct join is refused and its output deleted, blaming drift, when one part's container duration did not parse.** `expectedSec` uses `p.durationSec ?? 0`, and a part can reach it with `durationSec === null` **while still having passed `joinRefusal`**, because `videoEditListTrimSec` depends only on the per-track figures. It then contributes **0** to `expectedSec` and **its whole length** to `actualSec`. **He is told: *"joined file is 548.800s but the parts add up to 300.100s... Nothing was kept."*** — a false accusation and a deleted file. **The `?? 0` at `:297` points the opposite way from the deliberate one at `:344-349`**, which is the tell: one of the two is a considered default and the other is a reflex | open — High. **Exit: an unparsed part duration refuses the join before ffmpeg runs and says which part could not be measured — never after, and never as drift** | yes, not yet written |
| S63 | `lib/stitch.ts:343-351` | **The drift window grows with clip count, so a dropped short clip passes — and the comment claims a bound the code does not have.** `tolerance = max(frame, frame × clips) + predictedReturn`, **both terms scaling with count: six clips at 30fps is ≈0.6s.** The comment at `:340-342` says *"after S34, no part can carry more than the reorder allowance anyway"* — **not what the code does:** `joinRefusal:202` compares the **per-track** figure and never inspects `container`, so `editListTrimSec` is unbounded, and **it is `editListTrimSec` that `predictedReturn` sums.** **Scenario: a 0.4s accidental tap-record clip is dropped by the demuxer, drift 0.4s against a 0.6s window, the check passes, and `beats.json` records "6 clips joined end to end" for a file containing five.** **Read with S35, which is the same constant from the opposite end** — that row's reported premise was that the window is too *tight* and never reproduced; this is a measured demonstration that it is too *loose* and fails open. **Both cannot be fixed by moving the number; the shape has to change** | open — High. **Exit: a dropped part is detected regardless of clip count, and the comment's claim either becomes true or goes** | yes, not yet written |

Per the 27-row enumeration it becomes `decidedBy` on the **0.00-score `unsure`** case — **the most ordinary case there is, where the rule has least to say and says the most.** COPY's family with a score attached | open — Medium. **Exit: the third branch says what it actually knows — that it could not tell — and does not score as though it could** | yes, not yet written |

Fixtures already on disk | open — High. **Exit: the verifier's floor is never below the detector's threshold, and the four E9 spans report clean** **HALF DONE 2026-09-12, and the half that ships needs no audio. STILL OPEN — the clamp needs a Python change this seat cannot make tonight.** **The build is innocent, guarded rather than argued:** every span the build removed is reconstructed from `edl.json` and checked against the -28 dB map it cut against, and **all 37 are silence the map had already found**. Two forms, because the weaker one is not enough: coverage (every removal at least 95% silent; the thinnest measures 98.7%) and, sharper, **the longest contiguous non-silent stretch inside any removal is under 80 ms**, where a spoken word is hundreds. So "the build is cutting words in half" is false by the detector's own map. **A correction to the first instrument, recorded because it would have been loosened instead of fixed:** single-region containment was tried first, and one real removal (`im-telling-ladies-3`, 1119.362-1120.575) failed it while being 98.7% silence — it spans TWO adjacent silent regions separated by a **15 ms** tick, and trimming across that is what pause trimming is for. An assertion calling it a defect would have been given slack until it passed. **Not fixed: the floor clamp.** `verify_removals.py` is under `ugc-edit-system/` and takes only `--project` and `--margin`, so the app cannot pass the clamp in from outside — `--margin` shifts the floor relative to measured speech level, a different quantity from the map's absolute threshold. A structural test asserts the floor is still unclamped, so it goes red when the fix lands and this row can close on evidence. **And before it is prioritised again: `verify_removals.py` is not invoked from `lib/`, `app/`, `scripts/`, `tests/` or `native/` at all** — a hand-run diagnostic, so its false alarm reaches a person at a terminal, never Kayer. ## Client — `app/**/*.tsx`

None of these are testable yet: there is no DOM harness in the repo. Adding one
(`jsdom` + a render helper) would make C2, C8 and C16 straightforward, and is
worth doing before fixing them. **One row the harness cannot retire: C20** —
see its own note.

**Amended 2026-09-11: "not testable yet" was doing too much work.** C22 was
fixed and proved with no harness and no jsdom, by lifting its decision out of
the effects into `lib/follow.ts` and asserting on the decision. The claim worth
proving was never "the page did not move" — which needs a layout engine — but
*"no scroll was requested"*, which is a pure function of what the page knew at
the time, and is the stronger statement besides. Before queueing M2 for a
client row, ask what the row's claim actually is: where the defect is a rule
applied to the wrong inputs, the rule can come out and be tested on its own,
the way `lib/timelineLayout.ts` already holds the timeline's arithmetic. A
harness is still the answer for anything whose claim is genuinely about what
renders (C2, C8, C16, C21).

**Line numbers audited 2026-09-10.** 17 of the 19 rows citing one had drifted;
only C14 and C22 were still accurate. All corrected below. If you are reading
a client row written before this date somewhere else, distrust its line.

**Discarded from the 2026-09-12 nightly, recorded so it is not re-filed.** Its
**BUG-006** claimed `reorderTo` returning `null` when `to === from` is a defect.
**Refuted: it is deliberate and pinned by `tests/reorder.test.mts:28-29`** —
"into its own gap" and "into the gap just after it" both assert `null`.
Specified and covered. **Its own "needs clarification" label was right and its
defect framing was not**, which is exactly the distinction C38 was filed to
honour. One factual slip in its steps: it names a 4-pixel threshold;
`Timeline.tsx:460` is `MOVE_THRESHOLD = 6`.

**Two things that CLEAR that run rather than condemn it**, since one finding was
refuted and one of its passes was false. **No finding of its six or its
twenty-four rests on "a cut should not land mid-sentence"** — looked for
specifically, given Kayer's splicing technique, and absent. **And
fixture-dependence clears all six**: each was checked against the code path
rather than its container's tree. **Its credibility checks out against disk:**
`qa-screenshots/2026-09-12-nightly/` holds 38 files against a claimed 37 PNGs,
`_to_delete/` is 8.9 MB against a claimed 9.3 MB, and HEAD has moved past the
`5829ecc` it read. **It read the right tree and did what it said.** **One
arithmetic coincidence defused before it confused anyone:** its *"143 fixed, 12
still open"* is a **test** count and the ledger independently has **143 open
rows** — same number, unrelated quantities.

### Root causes — the dispatch unit

The 22 rows are **six jobs**. The ids stay (each has its own exit condition,
and they are referenced from commits, branches and every prior QA run), but
nobody should be handed one row of a group in isolation — that is how you get
six half-fixes of one rule. Dispatch by group; verify by id.

| # | The one rule | Rows | Where it is broken |
|---|--------------|------|--------------------|

| C28 | `lib/follow.ts:followScroll`, `review/page.tsx:438-452` | **CG1's other half: not whether to follow, but how far. The player leaves the window 3.3 seconds into playback and never comes back.** C22's fix holds for three of its four triggers — paused is **0px drift** across 37 samples over 31.4s, row-click holds at 876px across 25 samples, and opening a trim row grows the page 2958→3267 with scroll held at exactly 876px. Press play and the stage is gone: `0:00.0` scroll 0, stage screen-y `268..729`; `0:01.2` scroll 402, stage `−183..278`; `0:03.3` scroll **853**, stage `−634..−173`; `0:54.7` scroll 853, unchanged. Window is screen-y 93–860, so from 3.3s the stage's **bottom** edge sits 266px above the top of the window for the remaining 51 seconds. Same in **Rendered file** mode (px 1016, stage y −750). After a wheel it is worse and compounds as playback runs: destinations 853, then 807, then **1421**. Reproduced in **Chrome against the packaged app's own port** on a 2238×1371 viewport, so it is the review page and not the WebKit wrapper. **Mechanism:** `followScroll` computed a *destination* — `rowTop - (stageBottom + 12)`, "put the line just under the player" — and handed the difference to `scrollBy`. A destination is not a distance: twenty lines in, the row is 1,700px down the page, so the scroll is 1,700px and the player it was being carried to goes with it. Once the stage's bottom edge is negative, C22's own precondition refuses to follow again — hence *pinned*, not oscillating. `revealRow` was always right (↓ from the top moves 0→839 and puts the row at the bottom edge, minimum movement, `block:"nearest"`); playback following was the one caller that never learned it. **This is also the tester's Part 4a** — wheel, player visible ~4.8s, then a jump to 1421px at t=12.74s — so Part 2 and Part 4a are one row. **Why C22's suite missed it, which is the part worth keeping:** 5 of its 13 tests assert the *shape* of `page.tsx` (nothing reaches `window.scrollBy`/`scrollIntoView` outside `applyScrollRequest`). That assertion held and still holds. The shape was right while the behaviour was wrong — the executor was asked for the wrong number — so a source-shape test **cannot** catch this by construction. C28's file asserts values, and its last test asserts them **over a whole run**, because a runaway is a property of the loop, not of any request in it | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `C28-following-never-scrolls-the-player-away.test.mts`. Following now asks for the least movement that brings the line into view (the same rule `revealScroll` uses, in numbers) and is **bounded by the room the player has**: at most `stageTop` going up, at most `viewportHeight - stageBottom` coming back, so the sum of a whole run is bounded by where the player started and the runaway is impossible rather than unlikely. **C29b ruled by Kayer 2026-09-11: the conservative clamp stands.** He was offered the looser bound and declined it. So the player never leaves the viewport, and the price is that line-following stops after roughly three lines. **That price is to be recorded as a measured number, not as "a tradeoff"** — the tester has been asked for where following actually stops on his real project, and that figure belongs in this row when it lands. **THE NUMBER, measured 2026-09-11, and it is not "about three lines" — it is zero.** The player was wholly inside the viewport in **504 of 504** samples, so the promise holds exactly. But the page moved itself **exactly once**: 0 → 130px at t=1.21s on line 1, then held 130px for the remaining 151 seconds and 33 lines. `roomUp = stageTop` is 130px at rest and one request spends all of it; once the player is flush at the top there is no room left, ever. **The consequence was invisible in the estimate and is the part that matters: at his window size the lines list is never on screen at all during playback.** Player flush at the top puts row 1's top edge at viewport y 968 and line 34's at 2686 — **all 34 lines below the fold for the entire cut**. Seeing line 1 *with the player wholly on screen* needs a ~1010px viewport, i.e. a window about **1040pt** tall. **His primary display is 900pt.** It fits only on the 2560x1440 second monitor. The mechanism is alive, not dead code — at a 1360px viewport the same build followed four times (0 → 24 → 86 → 103 → 130 across lines 6-8) before spending the same 130px. **So: zero follow steps after line 1 at 1180x767; lines list below the fold for the whole cut; needs ~1040pt of window height to be otherwise.** Kayer ruled the clamp when the cost read "about three lines"; he is entitled to see this before that stands. **DECIDED 2026-09-11: do not loosen the clamp — fix the layout.** Kayer had twice delegated this (*"go with best judgement"*, then *"continue until the app is done"*), so it was taken rather than held. Loosening buys line-following back by letting the player drift off screen, which is R4 — reported twice, closed by C22 and C28. The real problem is that a ~400pt player plus a timeline band plus 34 rows do not fit in 800pt of viewport, and **no scroll arithmetic solves a space problem**. The shape: **a compact "line being spoken" strip directly under the player**, so he sees the picture and knows where he is without the full lines list needing to fit. C28's bound stays intact and becomes irrelevant. **Quote this number, not the step count: the player and line 1 need ~1010px of viewport to coexist, against his 767.** That figure does not move with the project, where "zero follow steps" shifts with header height and beat count. Reversible, and he will be shown it when it lands. Built as **M3a** |
| C29b | `lib/follow.ts:followScroll` — the clamp | **A decision, not a defect. Do not "fix" it without an answer.** C28's bound keeps the player **wholly** on screen: following stops before the picture would be clipped at all. On a 1440×900 window with a ~460px player that is about **three lines** of budget, after which the page comes to rest with the player flush at the top and the spoken line drifts below the fold for the rest of the cut. The alternative is to let the player slide up while its bottom edge stays in the window (`roomUp = stageBottom` rather than `stageTop`): about **eleven lines** of following, at the cost of the top of the frame being cut off — on a talking-head shot, the face. Neither keeps both promises for a whole cut, because a ~460px player plus the timeline plus a 34-row list does not fit in a 767px window; the thing that would is a lines list that scrolls in its own pane, which is a redesign and nobody's to start unasked. Shipped the conservative one, because the sentence this rule exists to prevent is "the cut plays where he cannot see it". One line either way: `lib/follow.ts`, `roomUp` | **needs a ruling** |
| C29 | `review/page.tsx:1241-1250` (the clock), `:1537-1542` (the duration), `:1255`, `:1109`, `:1459`, `:2046` | **CG4, and the whole call to action reads as never played.** In **Your cut**, the transport reads `1:38.0 / 2:06.8` and the timeline header reads `34 clips · 1:38.0`. The tester checked four sources rather than trusting the card: queue card `cutSeconds: 98, cutStale: true`; `work/edl.json` 52 segments summing **98.249s**; the rendered `img-9817-v6.mp4` `mvhd` **98.389s**; the live edit (`beats.json`, 34 spans, 130.20s − 3.37s of trims) **126.83s = 2:06.8**. So the two numbers either side of the slash were the **edit** and the **last render**. Cumulative beat arithmetic put the stop inside line 27 (`swear by this and are glowing up because of…`), making lines 27-34 — `So I'll get you that sale link`, `But just do yourself a favor`, the entire CTA of a product video — read as never played. **Mechanism:** `layout()` prefers the EDL's durations wherever the EDL still fits the beat, and is right to: `build_cut` snaps every edge inward against the silence map, so the rendered file is genuinely shorter, and those lengths are what keep the filmstrip lined up with the rendered picture. In **Live edit** nothing rendered is playing — the source runs through the beat ranges as they are now, and not one frame has been through build_cut's snapping — so the EDL describes a video that is not on screen. Replayed against the real project, **32 of 34 beats still fit their EDL pieces and the layout totals 98.084s**, which is the 1:38.0 exactly and the ceiling the clock could not pass. **Second half, same mismatch:** inside a line the clock clamped a SOURCE offset against a CUT duration (`min(c.dur, srcTime - c.start)`, two units in one expression), so on the real line 27 — 13.16s of footage rendered as 1.98s — the readout froze for 11.2 seconds while the picture played on. **This is not two defensible numbers to choose between.** `:1526-1536` had already settled it in its own comment ("it reports the edit now") and `cutDuration` obeyed; the clock, the scrub mapping, ⌘B's hit test, the span-delete resolver and the timeline the playhead is drawn on were the four call sites that never got the same treatment | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `C29-live-clock-runs-on-the-live-edit.test.mts`. One `cutLayout` for the screen whose basis follows the mode, and `lib/timelineLayout.ts:liveClock`, which **takes no `edl` parameter at all** — the omission that caused this cannot be made again by whoever adds the fifth call site. The clock goes through the pieces now, like `cutToSource` in the other direction, so holes come out of it. **One visible consequence, deliberate and disclosed:** in Live edit the filmstrip is always the last render against clips on the edit's clock, even on a fresh build, so `pictureStale` is now true whenever `liveMode` is — the badge that already says exactly this was simply never shown for the case. **Verified in the packaged app 2026-09-11: 504 samples over one uninterrupted run**, denominator `2:06.8` on all 504, numerator ending `2:06.8`, the run reaching **the end of line 34**; the old `1:38.0` ceiling appears nowhere and both sides of the slash are on the live-edit basis. Line 27 — the 13.16s line whose readout used to freeze for 11.2s — advanced `1:23.6 → 1:36.5` while `currentTime` advanced `428.447 → 441.398`: one second of clock per second of picture, for thirteen seconds. Line 8, three holes totalling 3.34s, shows a clock/source rate of 0.436 — the clock steps over holes. Native window (228 AX samples) and dev server (497) agree. Both surfaces. **Kayer was offered a change here too and declined it 2026-09-11, so the always-on badge stands.** Noted for whoever next works this surface, not filed as a row: **a badge that is on for the whole of Live edit is a badge he will stop reading inside a week** — the precedent is T4's "a suite was SKIPPED" warning, which fired on every run and was therefore read on none. Revisit when someone has actually used it; there is no point arguing it from a chair |
| C30 | the Live edit transport, below `.stage` | **The Play/Pause button receives no mouse clicks.** `document.elementFromPoint` down the button's own centre line — the button spans y 644-674 — returns `SECTION.` at 646, `DIV.tl-bar` at 650-662, and `SPAN.tl-stale` at 666-670: **not one pixel of it is topmost.** Five real clicks did nothing. `AXPress` starts playback instantly, and both Space and the "Your cut" button work, so **the handler is fine and the hit area is not**. Reproduced at five viewport sizes, on two projects, on **both** surfaces — not a WebKit quirk. Hiding the stale badge, then `.tl-bar`, then the whole timeline did not free it: the following section covers the gap regardless. Diagnosis: the transport renders in the gap below `.stage` (stage bottom 634, transport 635-682) while the next section is laid out as if the stage ended where the transport begins. **Adjacent observation, possibly the same bug rather than a neighbour:** on a fresh review screen the transport's geometry reports ~49px off from where it settles once video metadata lands and the player box resizes — it cost the tester two false starts, and a person sees the player twitch once on open. **This is what holds M-app open.** The tester graded it High, not Critical, and said it "does not block the exit line" because the cut is watchable via Space — but the exit line is *open a cut in the native window, **press play**, watch all of it*, and closing it while pressing play does nothing would be closing on a technicality that passed for the wrong reason. That is what M-app was reopened for once already | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `C30-play-button-is-hit-testable.test.mts`. **The diagnosis above is wrong and is kept because it was the reasonable reading of the evidence; the measurement contradicts it.** Nothing covers the button. `document.elementsFromPoint` at its centre does not contain it *anywhere in the chain* — `["DIV.tl-bar","DIV.tl","DIV.tl-wrap","SECTION","MAIN.main","DIV.shell","BODY","HTML"]` — which is also why hiding the stale badge, then `.tl-bar`, then the whole timeline freed nothing: there was never anything on top to remove. The button was **clipped**, not covered. `.stage .player` carried `aspect-ratio` on the whole box, fixing its height before any child was laid out; the `<video>` at `height: 100%` then filled that height exactly, so `.live-transport` — the next flow child — began 1px past the bottom edge and was clipped away by the `overflow: hidden` on that same rule. Clipped content paints nothing and hit-tests as nothing while `getBoundingClientRect()` still reports where it *would* have been, so the button measured present, answered `AXPress`, and took no click. That phantom rect lands in the gap between `.stage` (bottom 627.5) and `.tl-wrap` (top 641.5), which is what made it look like the next section had moved up over it. **The ~49px is the same defect, not a neighbour:** `scrollHeight 509 − clientHeight 460 = 49` is exactly what was being clipped (the transport's 47px, its 1px border-top, a pixel of rounding), and it is 0 now. **Fix:** the ratio moves off the box and onto the picture (`app/globals.css` `.stage .player` / `.stage .player video` / `.stage .player .empty`), so the player box has no imposed height at all and is the sum of its parts — a second control added beside Play cannot be laid out outside it. **Proof:** a presence test, a handler test and a source-shape test all passed while this was live, so the assertion is `elementFromPoint` — *the topmost element at the button's centre is the button* — measured in WKWebView at two viewport sizes × five player widths, plus `clippedPx === 0` and `.stage` ending at or below the transport. It fails on the pre-fix stylesheet with the tester's own `DIV.tl-bar` in the message |
| C31 | `.player { overflow: hidden }` vs `.empty` | **C30's class re-entering through a different child.** C30 moved the aspect ratio off the box; `.empty` now carries the ratio while `.player` keeps `overflow: hidden`, so a tall empty state overflows exactly as the transport used to — clipped **93px** at the 120px player floor, **27px** at 160px, clean at 180px and above. **Two things narrow it sharply and are why it did not hold M-app:** the native window has a hard 900px minimum, which floors the player at 196px, so **viewport width alone cannot trigger it in `SnipAi.app`**; and it additionally needs a project whose footage is in iCloud *and* untranscribed. Reachable only by dragging the resize grip below 180px. **Tester's own caveat, kept:** the pixel counts come from a hand-built fixture, so "27" is ±a few px while the threshold and direction are solid. **Take the tester's fix, not the obvious one:** the durable change is removing `overflow: hidden` from `.player` (or giving `.empty` a `min-height` from its content rather than a ratio), which makes the 93px case *and any future control added beside Play* disappear together instead of being caught one control at a time. Same argument dev made for putting the count check inside `ourListeningPIDs` where no permissive version remains — fix the class, not the instance. **Not its own slot:** cheap enough to fold into whoever next opens that stylesheet | open — low |
| C32 | `review/page.tsx:1192`, reached only from `:1747` | **The Trash button tells him his video was sent back for fixes. It was binned.** `toast(status === "approved" ? "Approved — ready to post" : "Sent back to re-cut")` — and the non-approved branch is **only ever reached by Trash**. So: click Trash, the app says *"Sent back to re-cut"*, no job is queued, `review-state.json` records `trashed`, the dashboard greys the card and drops it from the live count. He is told his work went for a re-cut when it has been thrown away. **And there is no way back** — no button anywhere produces `unreviewed`, so an accidental Approve cannot be undone from the UI either. Destructive, mislabelled, and irreversible, which is the worst combination of the three. **Not H2** — H2 is the missing `needs_fixes` verdict; this is a live lie about a destructive action, and it can cost him a finished video today | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `verdictCopy` + `C32-trash-says-what-trash-did.test.mts` (7 tests). **Two of the row's three charges do not survive measurement, and the fix is smaller because of it.** `review-action` writes one field, `s.cutStatus = "trashed"`, and returns: the test builds a project with beats, transcript, a cut and the raw `source.mov`, trashes it, and finds all four still there. Nothing is deleted and no job is queued. **`cutStatus: "trashed"` and the `.trash` folder are two different mechanisms wearing the same word** — only `DELETE /api/projects/[project]` moves a directory, and `lib/trash.ts`'s `RETAIN_DAYS = 5` and "Put it back" apply to that and only that. `listTrash()` does not see a trashed project, asserted. So the five-day restore does **not** cover this, and the copy may not borrow its safety. **Nor is it irreversible:** the card stays a link, and Approve writes a status `dashboard/page.tsx:35` counts as live — asserted both ways. What is genuinely one-way is `unreviewed`, which no writer anywhere produces. **So: wrong sentence over a defensible action, and the honest sentence is milder than the row feared.** Trash now says *"Trashed — off the queue. Nothing was deleted; Approve puts it back."* **Cause, not symptom:** the ternary was two branches over a four-valued enum, and its own comment said why — the third verdict was removed and its sentence stayed on the `else`, where Trash inherited it. The copy moved to `lib/verdictCopy.ts`, one entry per status in an exhaustive switch, so a fifth status is a compile error rather than a silent inheritance. **Proof that is not a proof-read:** each entry *declares* what its sentence claims (work queued / queue left / files erased / restore offered) and the test checks every claim against what the route measurably did to a real project in a temp library. "Sent back to re-cut" fails because no job exists, not because of how it is worded — so the next sentence that promises something the route does not do fails the same way. This is the C22 trap avoided deliberately: no assertion here compares a string to itself |

So the fix is to stop claiming otherwise and point at where quality can actually be judged — not to put the original on the live path | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `lib/playerCopy.ts` + `C35-the-note-names-the-file-that-is-playing.test.mts` (8 tests). **Wrong sentence, not wrong behaviour** — the row was right about that and the fix holds the line: the first test pins the proxy ON the live path, so this cannot be "fixed" by reverting it. **Cause:** the src and the caption were two independent expressions over the same field, thirty lines apart, and they drifted. So "Rendered file is already full resolution" is true of img-9817-v6 and **not true of any project built by the auto run** — see the proposed row below, because pointing him at a 720p render as the quality check would have been C35 again one surface along. *"The one that ships"* is true of that file at either size. **A second instance in the same expression, found by the sweep:** the live branch was not gated on `hasSource`, and `playFrom` (keyboard, unlike the disabled "Your cut" button) can enter Live edit without it — so with the footage still in iCloud the caption announced the original footage over an empty `<video>`. | C21 | Settings → Editing preferences, **Add a rule** | Clicking **Save** with the field empty does nothing and says nothing — no rule, no toast, no validation, button not disabled. Violates the cross-cutting rule that an action changing nothing says so | open |
| C22 | `review/page.tsx:408-417` | **The page scrolls itself while your hands are off the mouse.** Reported by Kayer 2026-09-11 in his own words — "no more scrolling when I'm not scrolling" — and this is the second time: the `pointerDown` guard at `:429` and `:531` exists because of the first report, quoted in its own comment ("The screen won't stay put. I cannot work this way."). That guard fixed dragging and does not cover this, because a wheel or trackpad scroll is not a pointer-down. **Mechanism:** `yield4s` correctly hands the list over when you scroll, then four seconds later does **not** merely resume following — it fires `setFollowTick`, which actively pulls the view back to the spoken line. Its own comment says so: "pull the list back now, rather than waiting for the next line". So the sequence is: you scroll to read something, you stop, you read it, and four seconds later the page moves on its own. **Second half:** the follow effect at `:427` checks `spoken` and `followRef` but never whether the video is actually **playing**, so a paused project does this too, where following a "spoken" line means nothing at all. A video player hands back the controls by ceasing to hide them, not by seizing the view. **Marked `regressed` 2026-09-10, not `open`** — `yield4s`/`setFollowTick` was introduced by commit `bb4bd5f`, which describes it in its own message as a *feature*. This is not an old defect nobody got to; it is behaviour that was deliberately added and that Kayer then reported, for the second time. Read it that way when scheduling it. **Now CG1, and it absorbs C19 as its third trigger**: (1) wheel/trackpad scroll, then `setFollowTick` yanks the list back four seconds later; (2) the same while **paused**, where "the spoken line" means nothing; (3) clicking a beat row, where `:529-538`'s `scrollIntoView({block:"center"})` centres the row and pushes the player and timeline off screen, because the `pointerDown` guard is already false by `click` time. One rule, three call sites (`:437`, `:536`, `page.tsx:364`), one guard that is wrong for all three. **Reproduced on the native surface 2026-09-11, with numbers, and it is now the sole blocker on M-app's exit line.** Scroll the player into view and within **~4 seconds** the page drags itself back to the lines list and the player leaves the viewport: `1.0 → 0.854` at the scroll, held through +3.67s, back to `1.0` by +4.32s. It runs while **paused** (`0.655 → 1.0` five seconds later) and after **F** clears selection (`0.0 → 0.541`). With a trim row open it snaps back in **under one second**. On a 1440x900 display a ~400pt player plus timeline plus lines cannot co-exist in an 800pt window, so playback continues with nothing visible — the cut plays and he cannot watch it. **C20 closed into this row**, and C22 inherits its wording: *"scrolling down from the player stops dead and reads as a freeze"* is what this yank feels like; there is no wheel swallow on the timeline band. **It also taxes the work:** it stole three of the tester's clicks in one session, one of which opened a Trim editor on his real project that had to be backed out of. **Proof does not require M2's DOM harness** — the tester proved it with AX scrollbar values, so this can be fixed and verified now. **Moved from M2 into M-app 2026-09-11** **A fix is in flight on `qa/c22-cg1` and is NOT reflected here.** This row was briefly marked `fixed` on 2026-09-11 by reading an uncommitted working tree — `lib/follow.ts` and a C22 regression file existed on disk, nothing was committed, no suite had been run, and dev had not reported. Reverted the same day. Flip it when the work is merged and the suite is green, not before  **DISCLOSURE, and it is the most instructive thing in this row — one of C22's own tests encoded the bug as its expected value.** `C22-page-does-not-scroll-itself.test.mts`'s *"the line still comes to the playhead"* asserted `req.top === 700 - (420 + 12)` — the overshoot arithmetic itself, written down as correct behaviour. Dev's words: *"That test was mine from the last run and it is the reason I believed the path was covered."* So C22 shipped green while C28 was live underneath it, and the green was **caused** by the test. That is the tenth sighting of tonight's pattern and the worst-shaped one: not a check that measured nothing, but the mechanism by which a fixed bug stays fixed, turned against itself. The assertion now checks only that following happens; the value moved to C28, where it is compared against measured geometry instead of against itself | fixed 2026-09-11 (`e761337`), with C28 |

Undo a delete in a reordered project and the line comes back in the wrong place.** `undoDelete` re-inserts at `beats.findIndex((x) => x.start > undo.beat.start)` — **source** order — but after a reorder the beat list is in **edit** order, and the two stopped agreeing. So the restore lands wherever source time says, which is not where the line was taken from. Undo that silently reorders the cut is worse than undo that fails, because nothing says it happened | open |

")` for `needsYourEye` — the split card simply does not | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `C37-the-seam-names-what-decided-it.test.mts` (8 tests). `Seam` gains **`decidedBy`**: each reason is recorded with the weight it contributed (weights untouched — only remembered) and `decidedBy` is the largest-magnitude one. `reasons` is ordered strongest-first too, so the list reads as an argument, but the tray reads the named field so a later re-sort cannot quietly undo it. Both sites render it. **The assertion that catches this is a relationship, not a sentence:** every reason was individually true and well-formed, so the defect is only visible BETWEEN two seams that disagree — *two seams with opposite verdicts cannot be explained by the same words*. With the sort removed the test reports *"both verdicts are explained by the same sentence: IMG_0062.MOV does not pick up where IMG_0061.MOV left off"*, verbatim from the persisted evidence. Its remaining duplicate proposal types are a row, not a detour. **Original exit, kept:** **Exit: the split card names the signal that decided the verdict (highest-magnitude reason, or all of them), and over the preserved corpus at `~/Movies/SnipAi/qa-m08/qa-transcripts/` each of the five seams shows a sentence that matches its own score.** **Confirmed in the packaged app 2026-09-11, and "shows the wrong reason" understates it — the row is rewritten to what was seen.** On a seam the rule got **right**, `reasons[0]` **argued against the verdict printed directly above it**: the card read *"This looks like one video"* with *"IMG_0062.MOV does not pick up where IMG_0060.MOV left off"* underneath. The reason that actually decided it — *"IMG_0060.MOV stops on \"you\", mid-sentence — that is not how a finished video ends"* — is in `batch.json` and never on screen. **So the tray can contradict itself in two lines on a correct answer**, which is worse than a wrong reason for a wrong answer: it invites him to override a decision that was right. **Third sighting 2026-09-11 on the three-clip run, and it is worse than "the wrong reason": `reasons[0]` is the SAME SENTENCE TEMPLATE above a join and above a split.** *"X does not pick up where Y left off"* served **two opposite verdicts** in one tray, with the deciding reason at `reasons[1]` both times and neither reaching the screen. Slice on a word boundary. **Ships with S38 in one commit** — rendering a flagged seam's confidence forces this card past `reasons[0]`, so they are one change and not a pair | fixed 2026-09-11, shipped with S38's plumbing |

She did **not** file it, because she cannot distinguish an app bug from an AX press that failed to set selection before the keystroke landed — and guessing would waste a fix. **That is the right call and the reason it is a row anyway:** deleting the wrong line is expensive, and this is one human click plus one Backspace away from being settled either way. **Whoever settles it does exactly that** — click line 13 with a real mouse, press Backspace, and report which line goes.
