## Native and shell — part of the app

| ID | Where | Defect | Status | Test |
|----|-------|--------|--------|------|
| N1 | `native/main.swift` (server adopt/health-check path) + the bundled `next-server` lifetime | **A sandboxed run can write into the real library, and did.** The app adopts whatever server already holds port 4737 instead of starting its own, and when it adopts one it **silently ignores `SNIPAI_DATA`** — the log says nothing about not having started a server. Killing the wrapper does not stop the bundled `next-server`; it keeps running and keeps the port, so the next launch adopts it. Reproduced: kill the wrapper, relaunch with `SNIPAI_DATA=<sandbox>`, and `/api/projects` serves the **other** library's projects. **Not theoretical — it bit on 2026-09-10:** `~/Movies/SnipAi/state/jobs.json` was written at **22:27** during a run that was supposed to be sandboxed, and `~/Movies/SnipAi/projects/`'s own directory mtime moved in the same minute. **Footage verified intact** — img-9817 (34 beats) and img-9823 (61 beats) both whole, both raw files byte-identical and dated before that night, all six cuts present; nothing was damaged. The mechanism that could damage it is live. This is the **second** time a sandbox boundary has leaked into his real footage by a different route (the first turned a `touch` into a write through a symlink), which is what makes it a pattern rather than an incident. Standing order 3 is "never touch the footage"; this row is the one hole in it, and **every future QA and tester run's isolation guarantee rests on it**. **Jumped the queue 2026-09-10 as M0.6** — see DOCKET. **Fixed 2026-09-11 (`3725b35`), with P19 in the same commit** — the port identifies nobody, so the server now says who it is (`app/api/health/route.ts`: data root, code root, pid, `force-dynamic`) and `native/LaunchDecision.swift` decides on that rather than on the port. A server serving a different library is neither adopted nor killed: the launch refuses and shows both roots. Every outcome is logged — no silent adoption. `startServer()` also passes `SNIPAI_DATA` explicitly instead of letting the server re-derive it. Reproduced live on port 4999 (not 4737): before, `serverIsUp() && serverMatchesDiskBuild()` were both true against a foreign-library server, so the old code adopted it; after, refused and the server still alive | **fixed 2026-09-11** (`3725b35`), probe 17/17, regression test green 3 of 3. **Whether the fix reached the artifact is M0.6's business, not this row's** — see the rows-vs-milestones rule in the header. If the rebundle shows it did not reach the app, this row goes `regressed`, which is what that status is for. **Two things from the fix worth keeping, because neither is in the defect and both are the night's thesis.** **(1) Ownership had to be redefined.** `next dev` re-titles its process to the **identical** string as the bundled server — not similar, the same text — so `ps` cannot tell our server from a stranger's at all. `process.argv[1]` survives the retitle inside the process, so ownership is now what the server *states about itself*, in three parts: it identified itself at all (silence is not ownership); the pid it names is actually holding the port, and that pid is the only thing ever signalled — never "whatever is on the port"; and it carries either this launch's `SNIPAI_LAUNCH_TOKEN` (a per-launch UUID, the only thing that can recognise a detached `npm start` in a dev checkout) or a `serverPath` equal to our own bundled server file. A dev checkout has no bundled server path, so a person's `npm run dev` can never be ours — **P19 preserved by construction** rather than by a second rule. One capability dropped on purpose: an orphan from a build older than `/api/health` is refused rather than restarted, which is no loss, because it was never actually restarted — that was the defect. **(2) A 44-check probe passed while the commonest path was broken.** The first `portIsOccupied` bound without `SO_REUSEADDR`; a gracefully-stopped server leaves accepted connections in TIME_WAIT on that port for ~30s with nothing listening, so the app **refused to launch, naming nobody, on quit-and-reopen** — the single most common thing a person does with this app. Only running the reproduction caught it. Fixed, and pinned by a probe case that arms TIME_WAIT deliberately. A unit test passing while the user-facing path is broken is M1's subject exactly, found this time in our own new code rather than in the old. Ownership now means what the SERVER says about itself**, because that is the only place the evidence still exists: (1) it must have identified itself at all — silence is not ownership; (2) the pid it names must be one of the pids actually holding the port, and that pid is the only one ever signalled; (3) and it must carry either **this run's `SNIPAI_LAUNCH_TOKEN`** (a UUID the wrapper mints per launch and passes to every server it spawns — the only thing that can recognise a detached `npm start` in a dev checkout) or a **`serverPath` equal to our own bundled server file** (`process.argv[1]`, which survives the retitle inside the process — an orphan from an earlier run of this same bundle). So `npx tsc --noEmit` has been checking `lib/`, `app/` and `scripts/` and **not one line of `tests/`**, for as long as the suite has existed. Confirmed here by reading the include set against the file list. **Unsoftened, because it belongs in the ledger rather than in a conversation that scrolls away: every "tsc clean" claim in this project has been narrower than it sounded** — the developer's all day, and the coordinating seat's when it repeated them to Kayer. That gate was being quoted as green and was never looking there. **Sized rather than guessed, which is what makes this actionable:** `**/*.mts` alone gives **148 errors across 58 files**, but **134 of them are one config error** (`TS5097`); with `allowImportingTsExtensions: true` it is **14 real errors across 7 files.** That is the job. **And the typechecker's very first complaint is the bug that had just been found by hand** — `tests/grouping.test.mts` and `tests/import-batch.test.mts` both missing `videoDurationSec` and `videoRawDurationSec` from `ClipProbe` — **and `import-batch.test.mts` is a second incomplete probe nobody had found, feeding `analyseBatch` -> `joinRefusal`, so S34's fail-open was reachable from two fixtures rather than one.** Also flagged: `tests/upload.test.mts` has four x *"Expected 1 arguments, but got 2"*, which may mean those tests are not calling what they think they are. **The gate to add, and its argument is the strongest on this board: the miss was invisible rather than merely unnoticed.** Nothing went red, nothing printed, and the check everyone quoted did not look. `guard-ledger` and `verify-bundle` both exist for misses somebody eventually noticed; this one had no symptom at all. **A check that fails when a file under `tests/` is not matched by `tsconfig.json`'s include set.** Note in the row's favour that whoever found it did not exempt himself — **one of the 14 errors is in his own S34 test file** | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `N18-every-test-is-typechecked.test.mts` (6 tests), `scripts/guard-typecheck.py`, wired into `scripts/test` beside the typecheck it is about (so `bundle-app`'s gate covers it). **`allowImportingTsExtensions: true` plus the `.mts` glob, then the 14, exactly as sized — no wider.** **The two that were the point:** the `ClipProbe` fixtures in `grouping.test.mts` and `import-batch.test.mts` now carry `videoDurationSec`/`videoRawDurationSec`, equal to the duration because measured on all eight of his real clips the video track is identical with and without its edit list. Dropped. **But `silent-noop-edit.test.mts` was more interesting:** it read `.changed` off `RangeUpdate` **without checking `.ok`** — precisely the call site S8's row said *"tsc, not review, is what stops a third call site reading `.changed` without having handled the refusal"*. **S8's stated protection was never in force for the tests.** It would have failed loudly rather than passed wrongly (`undefined !== false` under strict assert) but it would have blamed the arithmetic instead of naming the refusal; it handles the union now. The rest: `T7`'s env object needed a type, my own S34 file had an unhandled `null` from the fail-open fix (not exempting myself), and `tests/register.mts` needed a **narrowed** `@ts-expect-error` — `registerHooks` is Node 22.15+/24 and this repo's `@types/node` is **20.14.15, four majors behind the runtime** (`node -v` is v24.20.0). `@ts-expect-error` fails if the error stops happening, so a future bump removes it rather than leaving a stale suppression. **Worth its own row: the tests are now typechecked against an API surface four majors old.** **The gate found a 54th file on its first run** — `tests/native/fake-occupant.mjs`, an 88-line fake server N8's launch probe drives and nothing was checking. Hence the `.mjs` glob too. **It asks `tsc --showConfig` rather than reimplementing tsconfig globbing**, so it cannot disagree with the compiler it guards; import resolution is deliberately not used, because a test reachable only via a sibling's import appears in the program while still being outside the include set — which is the bug, not the fix. It states its denominator (T8/T15/T20's family), and refuses to pass when it finds no sources at all. **Non-vacuity: on the old include set the gate reports 54 of 54 test files outside the typechecker**, and the live-claim test goes red. **Original exit, kept:** **Exit: `tests/` is inside the typechecked set, the 14 errors are zero, and a new test file added outside the include set fails a gate rather than being silently skipped** | yes — `tests/regressions/N18-every-test-is-typechecked.test.mts` (6 tests); the gate is `scripts/guard-typecheck.py` |

**The COPY family — named 2026-09-11, sweep in progress.** Copy that
describes what the author was thinking rather than what the app does or what
to do next. **The same defect as a check reporting on something it did not
measure, moved out of the gates and into the interface** — and two of the five
known members are actively *false*, which is worse than useless, because a
person who reads "Sent back to re-cut" acts on it. Known members: **C32** (the
Trash toast), **H7(a)** ("appended to any Amazon link SnipAi puts in a
caption", where no caption surface exists anywhere in `app/`),
`lib/stitch.ts:186` ("Re-encode it before joining"), **P20**
(`~/Projects/SnipAi/.snipai.log`, absent on any machine without the repo), and
**N13** ("a port conflict is the most likely cause" — a guess, printed after a
30s build). New rows from the sweep should cite COPY so they are fixed as a
class. **C32 is not part of the sweep** — it keeps its jump and is dev's.

**Two-bases family, fourth sighting, 2026-09-11 — noted, not filed.** The
queue card read `8m 45s → 1m 38s` while the review header read
`34 clips · 2:06.8` for the same project at the same moment: the same
disagreement C29 just fixed, one screen upstream. The tester **declined to
file it** because the fixture's cut is a v1 render, which is the right call —
a row from a fixture that does not match the real case is how S21 and S23 got
sent to the wrong file. Check it on a real project; if it reproduces it is
S16/S24's family and belongs with them, not as a new id.

**Rough edges from the M0.6 tester run, 2026-09-11 — noted, not numbered,
so the count stays honest and the facts stay findable.** (1) Before refusing,
the app GETs `/dashboard` on the foreign server, because the build-id check is
eager and runs before the decision — read-only, but it **renders the queue of
the library it is about to declare it must not touch**, which is an odd thing
for a refusal to do. (2) The bundled quit's **success** path returns silently
after terminating by handle, so "I stopped my own server" is the one outcome
with no log line while every failure mode logs. (3) The bundled server cannot
be found by `pkill -f` on its path — a consequence of the retitle. Wherever a
tester is told how to clean up, the instruction is **kill by port**.

**Tester side effects declared the same run, none harmful:** the repo's
`.next` was rebuilt, so `.next/BUILD_ID` now differs from the bundle's —
harmless, since the packaged app reads its own, but **do not read that
mismatch as a signal**. `.snipai.log` was appended to by the dev launcher's
failed `npm start` attempts. Disk 12GB → 11GB, sandboxes deleted, port clear.

## Tooling

| ID | Where | Defect | Status |
|----|-------|--------|--------|
| T1 | `scripts/test:26` | A skipped suite still prints "all green". Note: the venv's `bin/python` is a symlink out to a system framework — it reads as missing from some contexts and is fine on the machine itself (`lib/paths.ts:30` documents this). Fix the reporting, not the detection | fixed — reports what did not run, and counts unittest's own skips too |
| T2 | `scripts/test` verdict line | Prints **"something is broken -- do not ship"** on a run with `# fail 0` and 173/173 passing. **Root cause found 2026-09-11, not T2 itself** — `scripts/guard-ledger.py` was setting `fail=1` on every run: it parses regression-test output for `✔ name`, the spec reporter's format, but its own `subprocess.run(capture_output=True)` pipes stdout, which Node never gives the spec reporter to — only tap (`ok N - name`). Zero matches every time, read as "cannot check the ledger," exit 1. **Fixed** — forced `--test-reporter=tap` and matched that format instead of guessing from TTY-ness. The verdict line itself was working correctly the whole time; it was reporting a real (if wrongly-caused) `fail=1` | fixed 2026-09-11 |
| T3 | `scripts/qa:124`, `:160-162` | One run, two opposite verdicts: nested section prints "something is broken -- do not ship", the run then ends "notes above, nothing blocking" and exits 0. CI reading the exit code gets a third answer. **Mechanism found 2026-09-11, and it is worse than filed: `--full` cannot fail on anything a guard reports.** `:160-162` runs all three guards with `|| true`, and `tests()` at `:124` decides purely on `grep -q "fail 0"` without reading the exit code — a grep that only ever sees node's summary line, while `scripts/test` sets `fail=1` for a `tsc --noEmit` failure, a guard failure, or a Python `unittest` failure, **none of which produce or suppress a `fail 0` line**. Demonstrated live: a run printed three ledger disagreements *and* a nested "do not ship", then ended "nothing blocking" and exited 0. So a type error or a red Python case makes `./scripts/test` exit 1 and `./scripts/qa --full` exit 0 — and `bundle-app:23-26` reads the second as its release gate. **The narrow half (qa honouring the exit code) is in M0.6's reopened scope**; the rest stays M1. **Fixed 2026-09-11**, with definitive evidence: same tree, same moment, master's `qa --full` printed "something is broken -- do not ship" in its nested section and still ended "notes above, nothing blocking" with **exit 0**; the fixed one exits **1**. `sweeps()` fixed in the same pass — the three guards ran as `python3 scripts/guard-*.py || true`, and the reasoning is worth keeping: *a guard that cannot fail the run is a comment*. On failure the section now prints `tail -40` instead of `tail -14`, because the cause is usually tsc at the top and the old window cut it off. **Not to be confused with T14** — `scripts/test:33` discarding the *board's* status is by design, since a board test written to fail must not gate | fixed 2026-09-11 |
| T4 | `scripts/qa` pipeline stage | `skipped -- no venv at ugc-edit-system/.venv/bin/python` in this environment, so the Python pipeline is never exercised. The script is honest about it, but a clean run says far less than it appears to. **Settled 2026-09-11: this is an environment artefact, not a defect, and it is out of M1.** Verified on the machine itself — `.venv/bin/python` resolves through the symlink, `Python 3.9.5`, `unittest discover` → `Ran 75 tests, OK (skipped=1)`. The suite runs. The one skip is `test_paths.py`'s copy-on-write test with a deliberate `skipTest("no shipped tuning.json to seed from")` — benign, and nothing to do with a venv. The "skipped — no venv" report comes only from contexts that cannot stat through the symlink into `/Library/Frameworks`, which is the phenomenon `lib/paths.ts:30` already documents. **The real residual, kept under a new description:** `scripts/test:60-61`, `:67-72` raises (corrected 2026-09-11 — **`scripts/test` only**; the `scripts/qa:129-131` half does not match `OK (skipped=1)` or `ℹ skipped 0`) "a suite was SKIPPED — 'all green' does not mean all ran" for that one permanent, harmless skip. So the warning fires on **every single run**, which makes it a warning nobody reads — and it is why M1's exit condition could never be met as written. Fix the reporting to distinguish a deliberate `skipTest` from a suite that did not run | open — **rescoped 2026-09-11**, no longer a venv problem |
| T14 | `scripts/test:32-38`, `scripts/qa:regressions()` | **`tests/regressions/` cannot fail a run — and that is general, not just for the rows kept red on purpose.** `scripts/test` parses the board into a coloured summary and never touches `fail`; `scripts/qa`'s `regressions()` pipes through grep/sed and never calls `bad()`. A live run printed `1 still open (14 fixed)` **in red** and exited **0**. The documented half is deliberate and correct: a board test written to fail must not gate. **The undocumented half is the trap — once a bug is fixed, its test joins the "fixed" count and still cannot fail a run.** Fourteen tests are in that state, including every one protecting an M0.5 or M0.6 fix. Concretely: revert `-movflags +faststart` from `lib/pipeline.ts`, `S19-concat-faststart.test.mts` goes red, the board prints "2 still open", `scripts/test` exits 0, `scripts/qa` exits 0, the pre-commit hook passes, and `bundle-app`'s QA gate ships it. With T7 (the board prints nothing) and T8 (the ledger guard sees 5 of 14), **no fixed bug in this repo is protected from returning.** The rule: a board test that has *started passing* must gate; one still failing must not. **This is also the real form of dev's proposed "the digest does not cover `lib/pipeline.ts`" row** — the ffmpeg argv at `:531-535` is the only place in that file where a byte of output is decided, it *is* already covered by S19's test, and that test sits on a board that cannot fail. Filed here with S19 as the named victim rather than as a digest-coverage row | open |
| T12 | `scripts/` manifest digest rule | **The digest covers the wrong `requirements.txt`.** There are two. `ugc-edit-system/tools/requirements.txt` is 32 bytes, dated Sep 4, unpinned, no `imageio-ffmpeg` line, referenced by nothing. `ugc-edit-system/requirements.txt` is the live one (`faster-whisper==1.2.1`, `imageio-ffmpeg==0.6.0`) — what `bundle-app:47` checks and `:97` pip-installs to produce the bundled ffmpeg. The rule is "every file directly inside `tools/`", so it covers the dead file and misses the live one, which is `tools/`'s **sibling** — the exact blind spot that produced N2. Failure: bump `imageio-ffmpeg`, rebundle, every subsequent cut is encoded differently, and the guard reports the manifest describes all 29 files as they are. The one inclusion made specifically because "the pinned ffmpeg decides what a frame looks like" points at a file that decides nothing | open |
| T13 | the manifest script's `--restamp`, and `lib/pipelineVersion.ts:41` | `--restamp` takes no argument and is the cheapest path to green: it rewrites digests while leaving `version`, `changed` and `why` untouched, so the manifest ends up asserting a date over a digest set stamped weeks later, with nothing recording it but a commit message the script cannot enforce. Require a reason and append to a `restamped: []` list. Same row, one-character fix: `lib/pipelineVersion.ts:41` names `./scripts/pipeline-version`, which does not exist — it is `.mjs` | open |
| T11 | `scripts/test:59-69`, `scripts/qa --with-pipeline` | **The Python half of the runner never sees T6's guard.** `scripts/test` shells to `.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover`, and `--with-pipeline` runs each `ugc-edit-system/tests/regressions/test_*.py` directly. Neither inherits the mutation `tests/register.mts` makes — that hook is Node-side only — `SNIPAI_DATA` is unset in that shell, and `ugc-edit-system/tools/_paths.py:20-28` carries the **identical** `~/Movies/SnipAi` fallback T6 exists to stop. Safe today only because the sole `data_write_path()` caller (`learn_from_edits.save():281`) is unreached by any test, which is precisely the property T6 was filed for losing. The gap is in `scripts/test`, which is in scope even though the module it protects is not | open | yes, not yet written |
| T15 | `scripts/guard-rebuild.py:92`, `:68-72` | **T8's disease one file over: a guard reporting on less than it implies.** `:92` prints `every edit handler schedules a re-render` **with no denominator**. Measured against today's `review/page.tsx`: its regex finds 53 `function` declarations, judges **19**, and **8 arrow-function `const` handlers are invisible to it** — so the sentence is true of 19 of at least 27. Whether any of those 8 mutates without scheduling is QA's call and has not been chased; the defect filed here is the claim, not the handlers. Separately `:68-72` prints `could not read <page>` and **returns 0**, so renaming that file makes the guard vanish while the suite stays green. Same subject as T8 and T14 — M1 | open |
| T16 | `scripts/check-launcher` on a non-Mac runner | **The no-skip decision meets an environment nobody reasoned about.** CI went on for the first time with the 2026-09-11 push (`4fdae88..df6ee3f`, 34 commits). `check-launcher` has deliberately **no skip path**, on the reasoning that no environment can legitimately produce this app and fail to compile `LaunchDecision.swift`. True on a Mac; **false on a Linux runner**, which cannot build the app and also legitimately runs `scripts/test`. **The fix is not "add a skip"** — a silently-permanent skip is exactly what T4 cost us, and N4 exists because this gate had no teeth. The decision to make is *what this gate means off-Mac*: either the Linux job does not claim to run the app's suite, or `check-launcher` distinguishes "cannot build here" from "did not compile" and says which, loudly, every run. **CI's actual result, 2026-09-11 — and the prediction was wrong in a way that sharpens the row.** The prediction was "no Swift toolchain". **Swift is present on `ubuntu-latest`.** It compiled `LaunchDecision.swift` and failed on Apple-platform APIs that do not exist in Linux Swift: `cannot find 'URLRequest' in scope`, `type 'URLSession' (aka 'AnyObject') has no member 'shared'`, `value of type 'HTTPURLResponse' has no member 'statusCode'`, `cannot find 'Darwin' in scope`, `cannot convert value of type '__socket_type' to expected argument type 'Int32'`. So the row is **not** "the gate needs a toolchain it lacks" but **"the gate compiles a macOS-only file on a Linux runner, and there is no third state between *did not compile* and *cannot be built here*"**. Dev's no-skip reasoning — no environment can legitimately produce this app and fail to compile this file — is true of every Mac and false of the runner, which legitimately cannot produce the app and legitimately runs the suite. **Also in that same log, and worth as much as the failure:** *"`tests/regressions/N4-launcher-rule-is-gated.test.mts` is on the board for N4 but reported no test result"* — T8's rebuilt guard catching its own gate failing to report, on the first CI run in this project's life. **The fix is still not "add a skip"** — a silently-permanent skip is what T4 cost us. Decide what the gate means off-Mac: either the Linux job stops claiming to run the app's suite, or `check-launcher` distinguishes the two states and says which one, loudly, every run | open |

The failure that reopens this: someone adds `.ts` to `lib/`'s relative imports — an obvious, desirable Node-native cleanup — or writes `import { PROJECTS_ROOT } from "../lib/paths.ts"` plus a `writeFileSync`, and runs it under plain `node --test`. **T6's own regression test will not catch that**, because every probe it spawns passes `--import ./tests/register.mts`: it tests the door already shut. (b) It currently sees exactly **S1, S2, S7, S18, S19** and is blind to 7 of 12 tests across 5 of 7 files — and the six tests the M0.6 fix added stay invisible **even after both regexes are widened**, because their titles carry no id prefix at all. That required switching from tap to node's **junit** reporter: junit is the only built-in that attributes a result to a file, where tap across several files emits one flat renumbered list with no filename in it. Per-file results make the same-id overwrite bug impossible **by construction** rather than by a tie-break rule, and a file reporting no result at all is called out instead of counted green. | T10 | `stop-snipai.command:28-34` | **Same "the port is our server" fallacy N1/P19 was just fixed for.** It runs `lsof -ti:4737 -sTCP:LISTEN \| xargs kill`, then escalates to `kill -9`, against whoever holds the port — a dev server, or any unrelated program that happened to bind 4737. Lower stakes than P19 because a person ran it deliberately, but the mechanism to do it correctly now exists: `/api/health` reports the server's pid and data root, so this can stop a SnipAi server and only a SnipAi server | open — **confirmed 2026-09-11**, and now trivially fixable: `/api/health` exposes `pid` and `dataRoot`, so the script can identify the process instead of assuming it. Dispatch with P19's surviving half — same fallacy, same fix **The product call dev refused to make, ruled 2026-09-11 (PM): `stop-snipai` stops what it owns, reports what it does not, and offers an explicit override.** Refusing outright is wrong — a person who runs a script called *stop* and gets nothing has been told the tool is broken. Killing blind is what N1, P19 and N8 were. So: SIGTERM the pid `/api/health` names as ours; if the occupant identifies as a **different** SnipAi (different `dataRoot`), say whose it is and stop; if nothing identifies itself, name the pid and the command and offer `--force`. The rule is the same one the launcher now follows — **we only signal what states it is ours** — with one addition, that an explicit human override may exist where an automatic one may not. Now ~5 lines on the shared `scripts/port-occupant.sh` | open |

| T18 | `scripts/bundle-app:92`, and `scripts/verify-bundle.mjs:70-71` | **The bundled Python's dependencies are never compared to `requirements.txt`.** `bundle-app` rebuilds `.build-cache/python-$PY_FULL-$ARCH` only `if [ ! -x "$PYBUILD/bin/python3" ]`, so **editing `requirements.txt` does not invalidate the cache** — the pins are read at `:97` only on the branch that never runs once the cache exists. `verify-bundle` then asserts only that `Contents/Resources/pipeline/python/bin/python3` and `.../bin/ffmpeg` **exist**, never what is installed inside them. | T19 | the suite's fixture helpers — `tests/regressions/_fixture.mts` and the `mktemp`-shaped sites beside it | **The test suite leaks fixture directories onto his disk, and it has been doing it for three days.** `/var/folders/.../T/` held **7,621 `snipai-*` directories, 273 MB**, accumulating since 2026-09-08 — created by the suite and never removed. **Cleared by hand 2026-09-11; the leak is the row, not the cleanup.** **Reproduced immediately after that clearing, which is the fact worth keeping:** one run left **75** directories, every one timestamped within the same minute (`snipai-c30-*`, `snipai-grouping-*`, and a stray `snipai-testrun-said-*` file). So this is not a historical accumulation to be swept once — it is ~75 per run, growing every time anyone runs the suite. **Worse than 273 MB suggests:** it is our own tooling quietly consuming the resource that is currently blocking Nadia's run, on a machine with 4.7 GiB free where the next test needs about 6.4 GB. **Retro question attached deliberately:** the fix is presumably one `try/finally` or a shared helper, but whoever takes it should find out **why the existing fixtures do not already use one** — several of these files were written after `_fixture.mts` existed | **fixed 2026-09-11** — `tests/scratch.mts`, `scripts/sweep-fixtures`, `T19-the-suite-cleans-up-after-itself.test.mts` (11 tests). **Exit condition met: 0 directories before `./scripts/test`, 0 after, `pass 225 / fail 0`** — against ~75 per run before. **The retro question has an answer, and it is the fix.** `_fixture.mts` — the shared helper, older than most of the files that leak — minted two directories and removed neither, so a fixture that wanted cleanup could not get it from the helper and had to hand-roll `mkdtempSync` **plus** `rmSync`. One of those is a line and the other is a lifetime problem, so what got written was the line. Audited across all **27** sites: **eight files removed nothing at all**, and most of the rest called `rmSync` as the last statement of a test body — skipped the moment an assertion throws, i.e. exactly when a run leaves the most behind. **So the helper was fixed rather than 27 `try/finally`s added:** `scratchDir(tag)` registers the directory and one `process.on("exit")` removes it, so teardown is not something a caller can forget to ask for. All 27 sites now route through it, `register.mts` included — its bespoke handler is gone, so there is one door. `process.on("exit")` was checked, not assumed: it fires for a passing file, a failing assertion, a top-level throw and an unhandled rejection; it cannot survive SIGKILL, which is what the sweep is for. **The sweep is a separate script so it could be tested against real decoys**, and its scope is the whole safety argument: `$TMPDIR` only, `snipai-` prefix only, real directories only, before the run only. **`-L` is tested before `-d` because `[ -d ]` follows symlinks** — a link named `snipai-*` pointing at a real directory would otherwise pass the guard, and a symlink is how a `touch` once became a write to his footage; the test asserts the TARGET'S CONTENTS survive. Marker files are deliberately left (register.mts explains the triple-banner reason) and a test pins that, so "directories only" cannot become a blind spot. **Two vacuity traps caught in my own test while writing it, both worth recording.** (1) `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT=child-v8` is inherited by a grandchild, and a grandchild that thinks it reports to a parent runner **exits 0 however many tests failed** — measured side by side, inherited 0 / stripped 1. The first draft did not strip it, so the passing-run test went green over a child that had not failed as intended. Every probe now proves it ran. (2) `SNIPAI_DATA` inherited meant the child never minted a sandbox, so the sandbox test passed with teardown deliberately removed. **Non-vacuity verified by disabling the teardown: 4 of the leak tests go red** **A third trap, and the reason the global count is measured from outside the suite:** `node --test` runs board files **in parallel**, so an assertion over a global `$TMPDIR` snapshot races against sibling files taking and releasing their own directories. The first version of the count test passed alone and then failed under `scripts/qa --regressions` -- a race, not a leak. Every assertion in the file is now scoped to its own tag or to a specific path it captured; the global before/after number belongs to a complete run and is recorded here rather than asserted from inside one  **The ruling that brought it here:** Medium. **Exit: `ls -d "$TMPDIR"/snipai-* \| wc -l` returns the same number before and after `./scripts/test`, and a test that fails mid-way still cleans up** |

That is precisely the ledger going stale — the one condition this guard exists to fail on — and **it is why this morning's column damage survived four commits and three "the ledger agrees" reports.** Same family as T8 (the guard's regex only saw `S` rows) and T15 (a guard printing a claim with no denominator), and the third time a guard has reported agreement about something it did not fully read. **M1's subject, arriving inside M1's own instrument** **BIT THREE TIMES ON 2026-09-12, and the third was a row about a guard — so this is now ruled ahead of S61.** Both E5's and S54's rows read *fixed* in prose and **`open` to the guard**, because the new status was appended after the old one and `cell[4]` still began `open —`. S54's additionally led with an uppercase **`FIXED`**, which the guard's **case-sensitive** regex cannot see at all. Corrected in `378ab58`. **So three malformed rows passed three green runs in one night.** **MECHANISM CORRECTED 2026-09-12 — twice, by the implementer, and two of the three causes this row asserted do not reproduce. Recorded the way S35's non-reproduction was, because a row about a guard that reports confidently on what it did not fully read must not itself carry a disproved cause.** **The real defect is an EXTRA COLUMN.** At `952d039` the **S34, S36, S42 and T19** rows each read `[fixed, open]` — two status cells — and the guard took `fixed` and **printed agreement over four rows whose status column said `open`.** That is narrower and more specific than anything this row originally claimed. **And the malformed rows were the PM's own**, from commits including `fadeb5e`: **I own this file's format, so a malformed row is mine regardless of which keystroke added the pipe.** **Tested and NOT reproducing, both now pinned by tests so neither can be restated from memory.** **(1) "A case-sensitive regex cannot see `FIXED`" — false:** `ledger()` lowercases the cell before matching, so an uppercase status was always readable; what produced *that* wrong reading was **appending a status after the word `open` in the same cell**, which the guard then read as `open` — **correctly.** **(2) "The guard can be satisfied by `fixed` appearing anywhere earlier in a row" — false:** `STATUS.match` anchors, so a row narrating *"fixed 2026-09-11"* mid-defect-column never registers. **That was my inference from how this ledger is written, and it was wrong.** **Third time tonight the reported cause and the real cause differed and the implementer caught it**, after S34's fail-open and S35's unreproducible premise — and the first of the three where the wrong cause was mine. **Ruled ahead of S61 on a specific argument rather than on the general "repair the instrument first" — which is now the fourth such ruling tonight and was due a check.** The test applied: **does the blindness compound, and is there a downstream signal that would catch the thing anyway?** **T20: it compounds with every row filed, and there is no downstream signal at all — a masked row simply stays masked.** **S61: it does not compound (each import is independent), and S54 landing gave it a downstream signal — a dropped clip now surfaces as a failure instead of being swallowed by a "done".** So S61 got safer tonight and T20 got worse | **fixed 2026-09-12** — `T20-a-row-with-two-statuses-is-not-read.test.mts` (9 tests), shipped with T21 because they are one fix. `ledger()` no longer takes the first status-bearing cell and stops: it collects every one, and **a row declaring two statuses is not a row to interpret — it goes to the unreadable list and is read as the most OPEN of its candidates**, so this guard can only ever complain more, never less. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THIS ROW, both measured rather than argued, and each would have aimed the fix at nothing.** (1) The regex is **not** case-sensitive in effect — `ledger()` lowercases the cell before matching, so an uppercase `**FIXED**` was always readable. What produced the wrong reading on 2026-09-12 was appending a status AFTER the word `open` in the same cell, which the guard read as `open` **correctly**: a malformed row, not a blind guard. That was my error and the diagnosis was mine too. (2) Prose does not satisfy it: `STATUS.match` anchors, so a row narrating "fixed 2026-09-11" in the middle of its defect column does not register. The row's stated mechanism — "the word fixed appearing anywhere earlier in a row" — does not reproduce, and a test pins that so it cannot be restated from memory. **The real defect is an extra COLUMN**, which is what four rows grew on 2026-09-11: at `952d039` the S34, S36, S42 and T19 rows all read `[fixed, open]`, the guard took `fixed`, and it printed "the ledger agrees with the bug board" over four rows whose status column said open. **Scope chosen by measurement, not preference:** of the rows in the live ledger, **12 carry more than one status-bearing cell and none disagree**, so this change flags nothing currently correct. The board subprocess then ran with the wrong cwd and **every board test reported as FAILING** — so the "prior guard" looked like it was catching the malformed row when it was really failing to find the repo. this is the **board** parse and the reporting channel — different function, different input, different failure. **Two rows, same family: the fourth instance of a guard reporting confidently about something it did not fully read**, after T8, T15 and T20. **Found by the 2026-09-12 nightly, verified independently**, including its ledger parse re-run offline at **143 open / 45 fixed / 6 wontfix** against the report's 141 — consistent drift, mechanism reproduces now | **fixed 2026-09-12** — tests in `T20-a-row-with-two-statuses-is-not-read.test.mts`, shipped with T20 because it is one fix: a guard that cannot tell a malformed row from a disagreeing one has T21's defect on the ledger side. `main()` did `wrong = list(board.problems)` and printed all of it under *"the ledger and the bug board disagree:"*. There are now two headings — **"this guard could not read part of its input"** and the disagreement one — and the unreadable findings print first. Both still exit non-zero; the difference is that the reader is sent to the filename or the parse rather than to the ledger's status column. Reproduced the way the row says, by renaming a board file, and asserted both before and after. A row declaring two statuses joins the same bucket (T20). **Deliberately NOT changed:** a malformed row may also appear under the disagreement heading, because read as open a green test over it genuinely is stale — the complaint was the two being conflated under one heading, not both being reported. **Exit: a guard that cannot read its input says so in its own sentence, and the ledger-disagreement heading is used only for an actual disagreement — demonstrated by renaming a board file** | yes, not yet written — renaming a board file is the test |
| T22 | `tests/import-batch.test.mts` — assertion/teardown ordering, and the whole file shares it | **An assertion placed before its own `clean()` teardown leaks its projects into every later test in the file, so one changed assertion took two unrelated tests down with it.** Found during S54's fix: the two additional failures were a **cascade, not a disagreement** — the surviving projects broke tests that had nothing to do with the change. **The trap is shared by the whole file**, so the next person to add or move an assertion pays it again and will read the result as their own bug. **Same lifetime-ownership class as T19** (the suite leaking 75 temp fixture directories per run): state created by a test outliving the test that created it, with nothing owning its removal. **The reason this is filed rather than fixed in passing: a cascade misattributes a failure**, and misattributed failures are how a green suite and a broken one become hard to tell apart | open — Medium. **Exit: teardown runs before any assertion that could throw, or the fixture's lifetime is owned by a helper rather than by statement order — and a deliberately failing assertion in one test leaves the rest of the file green** | yes, not yet written — a deliberately failing assertion is the test |
| T23 | `scripts/guard-ledger.py`'s `ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent`, and the `run_board` subprocess cwd | **The guard reports "your tests fail" when what actually happened is that it could not find the repo.** Run from outside the checkout — as it was when the old script was extracted to `/tmp` to build an A/B — `ROOT` resolves to **`/`**, the board subprocess runs with the wrong cwd, and **every board test reports as failing.** **Found by the failure it causes:** the extracted "prior guard" appeared to be catching a malformed row when it was in fact failing to locate the board at all, and that confident wrong reading survived **half an hour and three wrong theories**, broken only by instrumenting `run_board` directly. **Same family as T21 one layer out** — a tool reporting a *specific* verdict about something it never read — and the fourth guard-shaped instance after T8, T15 and T20. **The harm is not the wrong answer, it is that the wrong answer is plausible:** *"your tests fail"* is exactly what one expects a guard to say, so nothing about it prompts a second look. **AND THIS ROW IS THE HOME FOR THE GENERALISATION, 2026-09-12 — three instances now share that property exactly.** (1) This `ROOT` resolving to `/` and reporting the board as failing. (2) A `du -sh` misreading, where the wrong number was the one the instrument would plausibly give. (3) The nightly's two surfaces *agreeing* because they read the same variable (appended to S26). **The lesson is NOT "instruments lie" — it is that an instrument's PLAUSIBLE failure is the dangerous one.** An implausible wrong answer gets a second look for free; a plausible one is indistinguishable from the truth until something forces a control. **Which is why the control condition in PROCESS.md is a rule rather than advice** — and why all four guard-shaped rows tonight (T8, T15, T20, this) were found by somebody who happened to check, not by anything that would have failed. **A method note that belongs with it, because it cost most of the half hour:** an A/B built by extracting a script elsewhere tests the extraction as much as the script — twice the implementer concluded `run_board` had been changed by someone else when his own extraction regex had swallowed his constant. **The A/B now reverts only the selection inside the current file** | open — Medium. **Exit: a guard that cannot locate the board says so and exits non-zero on that basis, rather than reporting the board as failing** | yes, not yet written — running it with a cwd outside the checkout is the test |

## Edit quality — found by the looks gauntlet

| ID | Where | Defect | Status | Test |
|----|-------|--------|--------|------|
| E2 | `resolveSpanDelete` / `lib/timelineLayout.ts` | A span delete can leave a **surviving sliver of a line** rather than removing it. Found 2026-09-11 by `qa/gauntlet/looks.mts` from believable input: deleting 1.67-10.48s leaves `b2` at 0.170s; deleting 10.63-15.71s leaves `b4` at 0.236s (seeds 22075, 22077, 22084, 22097 — four in ~120). Two harms, and the second is the worse one. **On screen:** 0.170s is 4.3px at the default zoom (25px/s), under the ~6px a pointer can hit — you cannot grab it to fix or remove it without knowing to zoom in first, which is not a recovery path for damage you did not mean to do. **In the cut:** 170ms of a spoken line is not a line, it is a fragment of one syllable, and it renders into the finished video as a stutter. The arithmetic gauntlet passes all of these clean — `timeline.mts` asks whether the right number of seconds came out, which they did. Decide the rule: below some floor a remnant should be dropped whole rather than kept | open | yes — `qa/gauntlet/looks.mts`, category 022, reproduces at the named seeds |
| E3 | `ugc-edit-system/CLAUDE.md:30` | **The app scores his cuts against a rule the app itself breaks.** That line says "No captions, no graphic overlays, no PIP", and it is not a comment — it is the measured house-style target the scorecard's Pacing/style scoring reads. Meanwhile the graphics subsystem (~1,300 lines, six card kinds) is live and he uses it. Filed as bloat B2 on the theory one of them had to go. **Kayer decided 2026-09-11: the graphics stay, the rule was wrong.** So this is now a defect, not a decision — rewrite `CLAUDE.md:30` to allow overlays so cuts stop being penalised for a feature he deliberately built and wants. Before changing it, grep for every reader of that doc: the scoring path is the known one, and a house-style template built from reference videos also measures against it | open | yes, not yet written — assert a cut carrying graphics is not marked down for carrying them |

project owned them, so they were invisible to every run: no agent checked
them, no gate blocked on them, and the ledger did not know they existed.
`PROCESS.md` assigns them; `snipai-release` runs the checklist.

The remaining candidate is a **server-side cloud routine on Kayer's account**, which would explain every observation and is reachable only by him through the Claude UI. **Two defects, and only one of them is ours to fix.** **(a) The skill it was built to invoke, `snipai-nightly-qa`, does not exist**, so it ran an embedded fallback copy which its own header says will drift from the skill. Fixable locally in principle — **and deliberately NOT done yet**, because a skill whose only consumer is a process we cannot see, cannot invoke and cannot verify picked it up is a fix whose success is unobservable until the next 22:08, which is this project's own worst pattern: a thing reporting success about something nobody measured. **It needs the routine's prompt, which is his.** **(b) It selected its `SNIPAI_DATA` by fallback** — the named `qa-sandbox` had been deleted for disk, so it used `~/Movies/SnipAi/qa-m08`, **the tree holding the only corpus of Kayer's real speech**, and it generated a synthetic 185s `.mov` inside it. **That is N1's shape exactly: a process choosing a library at runtime with no owner.** **Credit where the run earned it, because it is why nothing was lost:** it opened `~/Movies/SnipAi/projects` **read-only**, never used his footage, had a delete request **refused by a safety classifier**, moved its staging to `_to_delete/` rather than removing anything, and **declared all five assumptions in its own first twenty lines**. Verified after: library **1023 files / 8.8 GB unchanged**, `qa-transcripts` **252 KB / 11 files intact**. **Nothing was harmed, and it was partly design and partly luck** | open — **and the row exists because it cannot be closed from here.** **Exit: Kayer locates the routine and either retires it or its prompt names an explicit `SNIPAI_DATA` that is not inside `~/Movies/SnipAi`, and the skill it invokes exists.** Until then the protective action is to move the corpus out of the fallback's reach — see DOCKET | no — nothing here can invoke it |

## Edit quality — the cut itself

| ID | Where | Defect | Status | Test |
|----|-------|--------|--------|------|
| E1 | beat out-point placement | Two beats end 20-30ms before their own final word finishes: `under-eyes-looks` leaves 26ms of `'this,'` past the out-point (mean -22.5dB, peak -12.4dB) and `egf-going-improve` leaves 21ms of `'face.'` (mean -22.9dB, peak -11.6dB), both above the project's -26.2dB speech floor. Both fragments sit **inside** their line, not past an abandoned take, so this is clipping and not take selection. `img-9823`'s two flagged fragments **are** take selection working and are not filed. Note `snap_tail` is **0.01** today, so this is not P2's 0.881 residue — the boundary placement itself is tight. **Traced 2026-09-11: not the boundary placement after all.** `edl.json` shows `snap()` already placed both out-points correctly, exactly on Whisper's own word-end timestamp (96.92, 164.02). The loss happens one step later, in `build_cut.py`'s per-clip ffmpeg extraction: `-ss <x> -i <source> -t <dur> -r 30 ...` truncates the re-encode to whole output frames, which can drop up to one frame (33ms at `-r 30`) off the requested tail — invisible when that frame was silence, audible when it held the end of a word. Both measured losses (26ms, 21ms) are under one frame. **Fix written, not render-verified** — `FRAME_PAD = 0.04` added to the requested `-t` duration (and threaded through the fade-out math so a fitted fade still lands on the true tail). Confirmed the corrected `-t` value reaches `extract.sh` (2.28→2.32, 3.22→3.26). Could not render a real sample tonight: pulling even 2.3s out of the 1.5GB source through this session's connection to the Mac timed out twice, likely that connection's own I/O to a large file rather than anything about the fix. Needs a real rebuild (which runs natively on the Mac, not through this connection, so should not hit the same wall) to confirm `qa/verify_edges.py` reports 0 of 34. **Confirmed by a real render 2026-09-10 — and the stated test was the wrong test.** `qa/verify_edges.py` reads `work/edl.json`, `work/transcript.json` and the source audio; **it never opens a rendered clip.** E1's truncation happens *downstream* of the EDL, in ffmpeg's per-clip re-encode, so the tool reports clean with or without `FRAME_PAD` — and did report clean on a packaged build that was demonstrably still clipping. It could never have proven this fix. What actually proves it is measuring each rendered clip's real duration against its requested `dur`: **dev, +0.037s to +0.059s across all 26 clips, 0 short.** The packaged app measured 3 of 7 clips short only because the bundle was three days stale and had no `FRAME_PAD` at all (see T5) — a packaging failure, not this one | fixed 2026-09-10 (dev-verified by real render; packaged pending the rebundle) | **replaced** — not `verify_edges.py`. Measure each rendered clip's duration against its requested `dur`; green when none is short |
| E4 | EDL boundary placement, on a joined multi-clip source | **Suspected.** Six EDL boundaries land inside words too short for the Whisper-timing explanation: `up` **0.58s**, `This` **0.44s**, `I'm` **0.36s**. E1's fix (`FRAME_PAD`, `WORD_RESCUE`) addresses a 20-30ms overhang at a beat's out-point; a boundary inside a 0.36s word is an order of magnitude away from that, so the standing explanation does not cover these. **Filed as suspected on purpose, and it is only checkable once S34 lands** — Nadia had 90-second excerpts, and the full clips are the joined ones | open — **suspected, needs a full-clip measurement.** **Exit: measure every EDL boundary on a joined project against its word timings — either the six reproduce (a defect with a named cause) or they do not (the row closes as an artifact of the excerpt, and says which)** | no — needs the measurement first |
| E5 | the beat drafter's take handling, seen on a joined cut | **Eight consecutive beats are eight attempts at one line, and the cut says it eight times across 24 of its 33.75s — 71% of the finished video is one sentence repeated, and none of it is marked `needs_review`.** For scale, the longest such run in his two real projects is **2**, so this is not a known level of repetition; it is what happens when the take picker meets how he actually shoots — *"he says each line over and over until he nails it, then stops recording after a completed take"*, which is S37's first mechanism from the same root. **The check that would have caught it ran and was not heard:** `verify_cut.py` detects repeated phrases, and S28 already records that its FAIL reaches no surface the user looks at | **fixed 2026-09-12** — **Exit: on the joined `{0060,0061,0062}`, either one attempt survives per line, or the run is marked `needs_review` and the review screen shows it** — measured on a real join, so it needs S34. **MEASURED 2026-09-11 on the real two-clip join, and the numbers are much better than the 90-second excerpt suggested — plus the second branch is satisfied.** Longest run of near-duplicate beats is **3, not 8**; runs total **26.07s of a 100.90s cut (25.8%, not 71%)**; and **both duplicate-attempt beats are flagged** — *"Repeats itself — two attempts at the same line are inside this take"* at 0.15 confidence — with the review screen showing `12 of 36 need a call`. So the row's own exit is met on its **second** branch: the run is marked and shown. **What remains is the first branch and it is Part 5 #10's known gap** — the repetition still reaches the finished file. **Kept open at Low rather than closed**, because the exit was written as either/or and the either/or was satisfied honestly; it stays as the place the known gap is tracked against real numbers **RE-WEIGHTED 2026-09-11 from Low back to High, and the row says plainly that its severity was assessed against the wrong yardstick.** Tonight I downgraded this to Low because both duplicate beats were correctly flagged — judging it as a *correctness* row. Against Kayer's stated target of **about a minute**, the same measurement reads completely differently: **26.07s of near-duplicate beat runs is not a 25.8% blemish on a 100.90s cut, it is 43% of the entire video he wants.** Him saying the same line twice is the single largest recoverable block of time in any cut we have measured. **And the mechanism that should reclaim it already exists and is connected** — the take picker choosing the better of two attempts is exactly this job, and on the three-clip run it *did* choose across clips (98% over 90%), so the machinery works and is simply not being pointed at intra-beat repeats. **The first branch of this row's exit — one attempt survives per line — stops being Part 5 #10's known gap and becomes M0.85's principal lever** **Sharpened 2026-09-11 against the eight references, and the mechanism is now named rather than described: this is a BEAT-IDENTIFICATION failure, not a take-selection one.** `draft_beats.py`'s own definition of a beat is *"ONE complete recitation"* and it produced **eight beats for eight attempts at one line**. So the fix is not *pick a better take* — the picker demonstrably works, it chose 98% over 90% across clips tonight — it is **collapsing near-duplicate beats into one beat carrying many candidate takes**, after which the existing picker does the rest. **And this is where M0.85's length half actually lives:** the app keeps **16.8-19.1%** of his raw footage against the **20.3%** he kept by hand on his own reference pair, so it is already cutting harder than he does — the output is long because he records ~19 minutes of takes for a one-minute video **Confirmed as the lever from a SECOND independent direction 2026-09-11, and this one does not depend on any target length — which matters now that Kayer has withdrawn the minute.** Rhythm is measured and already correct: the app cuts at **22.6-28.1 cuts/min** inside his **17.0-28.9** range, with a **2.11-2.59s** average shot inside his **1.97-3.28s**. **So the surplus runtime is not slack pacing — it is repetitions of content**, which is `draft_beats.py` emitting eight beats for eight attempts at one line. **Collapsing them into one beat with many candidate takes reduces runtime without touching a rhythm that is already right.** And it is the same conclusion the kept-fraction arithmetic reaches from the other side (16.8-19.1% against his 20.3%). **Justified on repetition rather than on length, so it survives him retargeting the milestone** **WIDENED 2026-09-12, not re-scoped — and the distinction was established by asking him one more question.** Kayer described how he actually edits: *"I pick the strongest parts of my takes and stick them together with a small jay cut... if I have a strong first half of a sentence but then mess up and then I do another take where the beginning half sucked but I finish the sentence strong I'll cut and stitch them together."* **And then, asked whether that is his method: "I first take the best take. If I don't have a best take I do option two. It doesn't happen all the time but maybe keep in mind."** **So choosing the best whole take is his PRIMARY method and mid-sentence composition is option two, occasional.** This row's scope is therefore **correct and is the lever**: the app today keeps eight attempts as **eight beats**, so collapsing them to one line and picking the best **is** his primary technique and the app does not do it. Tonight's 98%-over-90% result is the picker working; what is missing is the grouping that lets it work per line instead of per clip. **Why the grouping half matters on its own:** the drafter emitting eight beats for eight attempts is wrong independently, because eight rows for one line breaks the lines list, the `needs_review` count and the review screen — **and because you cannot compose from candidates until candidates are grouped as candidates for the same line.** **So this row is both halves: group repeated attempts as candidates of one line, and let the picker choose among them.** Sub-take composition is **E8** and sits behind this one rather than inside it **FIXED 2026-09-12 — `lib/retakes.ts`, `E5-one-beat-per-line-not-per-attempt.test.mts` (15 tests).** A run of adjacent near-duplicate beats becomes ONE beat carrying every attempt, and the existing picker chooses. **No new selector** — the picker chose his 98% delivery over a 90% one unaided and this feeds it. **`draft_beats.py` was not touched:** it is under `ugc-edit-system/`, so the collapse runs in the app, in one helper both of the drafter's callers use, because a collapse on one of them would mean an import and a re-draft produced different beats. **Measured on the real joined project: 67 beats to 42, beat span 259.40s to 170.64s, 88.76s of repetition off 16 lines.** Estimated cut 183.27s to about 121s using the real EDL ratio (0.707). **Cuts per minute goes 21.9 to 20.9, inside his hand-cut 17.0-28.9** — asserted as a REGRESSION GUARD, with the test saying in as many words that moving outside his range would be a finding and not an improvement. **THE THRESHOLD IS MEASURED, AND THE FIRST CALIBRATION WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING.** Similarity is longest common subsequence over the shorter beat. Two alternatives were tried and rejected on his own data: longest common SUBSTRING scores the very pair the picker chose at **0.44** (a mid-sentence stumble breaks the run), and a plain sequence ratio cannot separate real retakes at 0.61 from two different benefit lines at 0.43. **The calibration was first done in Python with difflib, which is not a true LCS and scores lower — it put the weakest real retake at 0.61 where the shipped function puts it at 0.75.** The threshold survived the correction; the recorded margins did not, so they are now asserted rather than written down. Against a hand-read ground truth of 15 unambiguous retakes and 30 unambiguous distinct pairs: **retakes min 0.75, distinct max 0.43**, threshold **0.55** — 0.12 above the dangerous side, 0.20 below the other. Both directions verified by moving it: at 0.40 the distinct test fails, at 0.80 three retake tests fail. **AND THE CONTROL CONDITION, per the rule added to PROCESS.md tonight.** The hazard is that he films the same product repeatedly with the same formulas — topic overlap is what killed the grouping heuristic. The shipped function over **1800 pairs from DIFFERENT videos** in `qa-m08/qa-transcripts`: median 0.00, p90 0.20, **p99 0.40**, only **3 of 1800** at or above 0.55. So it measures repetition, not subject matter. **THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION IS PINNED FIRST AND EXPLICITLY**, because over-collapsing deletes something he said once and nothing flags it, while under-collapsing leaves a repeat the review screen already marks — only one of those errors is silent. Tests assert that no beat vanishes (every original is still a beat or a recorded take), that no distinct line collapses, and that the harm reproduces before any of it. **A REAL BUG CAUGHT BY THAT DISCIPLINE:** keeping "the last attempt" would have kept `chasing-them-kids-2`, which in his actual beats.json is **396.17 to 396.17 — zero length**, and is the last attempt in its run. That would have collapsed three attempts into a beat the builder renders as nothing, turning a repeated line into a MISSING one. The keeper is now the last **usable** attempt, which preserves 15.06s of speech the first version silently dropped. **Two findings for their own rows:** that zero-length beat is a drafter defect — his beats.json contains a beat shorter than the 0.15s minimum the app's own PATCH validator enforces, so the drafter can write a beat the app would reject. And beat 53 to 54 (*"I'm gonna link it down below if"* / *"you have any questions."*) is one sentence split across two beats with a complete retake following — the E8 shape, left alone. **Which attempt survives is provisional and the code says so:** the last usable one, because that is his described behaviour, NOT a claim it is the best. `candidates/route.ts` now searches the whole run rather than a fixed 8s window — his six goes at the powerhouse line span 818s to 888s, so the old window would have offered the picker one candidate and called it a choice. **The ruling that brought it here:** open — Medium today, **High the moment a joined cut is postable.** | fixed 2026-09-12 — collapse in `lib/retakes.ts`; the picker chooses among the takes | yes — `E5-one-beat-per-line-not-per-attempt.test.mts` (15 tests), measured on the preserved three-clip evidence |
| E6 | the export versus its EDL, on a joined source | **Export ran 1.29s longer than its EDL.** This is **S26's arithmetic, not a new mechanism**: the render adds `FRAME_PAD` (0.04s) per clip and the EDL sum does not account for it, so drift is `0.04 x clip count` and 1.29 / 0.04 is about 32 clips. **Filed as its own id because it is the first measurement of that drift on a joined source, and so the finding stays findable — but it is not a second fix.** It closes when S26 closes, and whoever takes S26 re-measures here | open — Low, **closes with S26.** **Exit: S26's exit, re-measured on a joined multi-clip project** | no — S26's test covers it |
| E7 | the pipeline's internal-silence threshold, against his eight reference videos | **The dead-air check permits something that never occurs in his own work.** The pipeline flags internal silence over **~3s**. Measured across eight videos he cut himself: **six of eight have zero internal gaps, five contain 0.00s of detectable silence**, and the two non-outlier non-zeros are **0.44s and 0.54s with the gap at the tail**. Tonight's cut shipped a **1.99s internal gap and nothing objected**, carrying **11.78s over 25 gaps — 6.2% of runtime**. **This is not a loose threshold, it is the wrong shape of rule: his standard is an absolute, not a budget.** **The cheapest half of M0.85, and he has proved it reachable** — fighting `img-9817` by hand over six versions he got to **1.38s / 1.4%**. **Do NOT simply set the threshold to 0.25s:** his tail gaps are legitimately his, `5ca5169e` is one of his own videos at 15% silence and a hard rule would reject it, and a pause before a punchline is not dead air. This wants a measured rule with his reference set as the yardstick, not one constant swapped for another **SPECULATION RETRACTED 2026-09-12 — he answered it plainly and the guess was wrong.** Two hypotheses were floated in this row's history: that his 0.00s internal silence was emergent from **where** he cuts (mid-sentence), then from **how** he joins (the J-cut). **Both are superseded by his own words: "If I'm saying a sentence I don't cut in between each word. I cut out dead space so there's zero dead air."** **It is a deliberate, routine, primary operation.** The retraction is recorded rather than the line quietly replaced, because the record should not carry our guess as a hypothesis about his technique once he has stated the answer.

**AND THAT MAKES THIS ROW MUCH SMALLER THAN IT LOOKED — possibly a constant rather than a feature.** **The app already removes silence**: tonight's cut carried **56 interior removals** which exist only in `edl.json`. The capability is not missing. **The app removes gaps above roughly 3s and leaves everything below; he removes all of them.** Same operation, one threshold apart — and that is the *entire* measured gap between his 0.00s and the app's 5.6-6.2%. **The warning already in this row is now his stated intent rather than our inference:** do **not** set a global 0.25s floor. He cuts dead space **inside a sentence's delivery**, and both of his non-zero references have their single gap **at the tail**. So the shape is a **much tighter interior threshold with the tail and deliberate beats exempt** — and `5ca5169e` proves deliberate beats exist, since he withdrew it as *"a deliberate pause for effect."* **Testable in the same script already ordered** — add one column: is speech continuous across his cut points? **AND A DISTINCTION FOR WHOEVER TAKES THIS, from S71/S72: the residual silence may be gaps NOT DETECTED rather than gaps detected and left.** The detector's level threshold is a hardcoded **−28 dB** (`silence_map.py:14`, no `--noise` passed), and his clip room tone varies by **15 dB** across one join — so on a hot clip a real pause can sit above −28 and never be found at all. **Establish which before changing the duration rule:** gaps-not-detected is a level problem (S72's territory), gaps-detected-but-under-3s is the duration problem this row describes. **Different fixes, and the measurement that separates them is one pass of `silence.txt` against the finished cut's own gaps.** | open — High, **M0.85's air half, pending the measurement that may reclassify it as emergent**. **Exit: a cut built from his footage has no unexplained internal gap, measured with `silencedetect=n=-35dB:d=0.25` — the same instrument used on his reference set — and the references remain the yardstick rather than a threshold we chose.** **Strengthened 2026-09-11 when Kayer withdrew `5ca5169e`: the standard is now an absolute with no exceptions — five of seven at exactly 0.00s, the other two a single tail gap each (0.44s, 0.54s).** No internal dead air is a rule he does not break. **TWO NOTES THAT PREVENT FALSE POSITIVES, and whoever takes this must read both.** **(1) He speeds up sections** — his own explanation of the withdrawn file was *"deliberate pause for effect. I was gargling mouthwash and sped it up."* **So a future reference reading 15% silence may be his technique and not a defect.** Confirmed by grep: **the pipeline has no concept of a rate anywhere** — no `setpts`, no `atempo`, nothing on a beat or in the EDL carrying a speed; every `rate` in the codebase is an audio sample rate for the waveform. **A high-silence reference is therefore evidence he did something the app cannot represent, not evidence the reference is bad.** **(2) Do not replace the median with a mean.** `app/api/references/route.ts:167` takes the *median of them all*, which is why the 15% outlier never needed removing in the first place — he removed it by hand as a bad example, and **if he ever drops a video like it into `inspiration/` the median absorbs it again.** The design did not need his correction; a mean would | yes, not yet written; the eight references are the corpus and no video generation is needed |

**DIAGNOSED 2026-09-12 by Theo, and the answer overturns the hypothesis. STILL OPEN — every lever is outside the developer seat's scope.** Mara asked which of two things the residual silence is before anyone tunes a number, and it is decisively **detected and kept**, not never detected. Measured on his own finished project from the M0.8 exit run (`qa-exit-0912/projects/img-0060/work`, `edl.json` against `silence.txt`, no audio needed): the **-28 dB map already identifies 22.48 s of silence sitting inside kept EDL pieces — 18.4% of a 122.24 s cut**, against Nadia's measured residual of 5.6-6.2%. Three times more is detected than reads as dead air, because the -28 dB map is generous about quiet word tails; against the strict -50 dB map it is 9.51 s / 7.8%, which brackets the measurement from the other side. Where it sits: head of a piece **11.34 s (9.3%)**, tail **4.54 s (3.7%)**, interior **6.60 s (5.4%)**.

**The cut's ending is the clincher, and it kills the developer's own first explanation too.** The final piece runs 2.08 s of which **1.77 s reads as silence at -28 dB and 1.65 s at -50 dB** — so even the strict map, the one edge snapping uses, sees it. The guess had been that the strict map could not see the tail; it can. The out-point simply was not pulled in, so the rule had the information and did not act on it.

**So the threshold is the innocent number, and S72's calibration route is aimed at the wrong thing** — making the threshold per-file cannot remove silence the map has already found. The lever is the **keep rule**: the pause trim and the edge snap in `build_cut.py`, plus the hardcoded threshold at `silence_map.py:14`. All under `ugc-edit-system/`. The one in-scope lever — `lib/pipeline.ts` invoking `silence_map.py` with no `--noise` — is the one the diagnosis says not to touch. **Guard: `tests/dead-air-is-detected-not-missed.test.mts` (5 tests), in the MAIN suite rather than the bug board**, because it passes today and the board is for tests written to fail until a defect is fixed. It pins the MECHANISM, which survives the fix, and leaves the amount recorded here: an assertion on 18.4% would have to be rewritten by the very fix it guards. It also pins that the app still takes -28 dB by omission, so a later `--noise` makes this diagnosis stale loudly.

| E8 | the edit model — `lib/types.ts:1-21` — versus how Kayer actually edits | **The app cannot express his fallback technique, and the blocker is a deliberate past decision rather than an oversight. Secondary and occasional by his own account — filed behind E5, not folded into it.** His technique, in his words: *"I pick the strongest parts of my takes and stick them together with a small jay cut... a strong first half of a sentence... another take where... I finish the sentence strong I'll cut and stitch them together."* **So one spoken line's picture comes from two different takes, spliced mid-sentence.** **Scope corrected within an hour of filing, by asking him:** *"I first take the best take. If I don't have a best take I do option two. It doesn't happen all the time."* **So this is real, it is his, and it is the exception rather than the rule** — which sets its priority without changing its content. **A `Beat` cannot represent that.** It is `start: number; end: number` plus `holes?: [number,number][]` — **one contiguous source region minus interior gaps, all from the same take.** **And the reason is recorded in the type's own comment at `:16-19`:** *"Cutting a bit out of the middle of a take used to split the beat in two, which left two rows saying the same line. It is one line with a piece taken out, so it stays one beat."* **That decision was right for the reason it was made** — one line, one row — **and its consequence is that his actual method is inexpressible.** **Recommendation on representation, framed rather than decided, because the invariant is load-bearing:** the requirement is *one line, one row, picture from a LIST of source spans* — a `pieces[]` on the beat, or `holes` generalised into a span list. **That preserves exactly the invariant `:16-19` was written to protect.** The alternative — two adjacent beats with a suppressed junction — **re-creates the two-rows-one-line problem this codebase already rejected once and wrote down**, so it should not be reached for without answering that comment. Theo picks the structure; the invariant is not his to trade away. **Also already present and pointing the same way:** `:5-9` documents `audioStart`/`audioEnd` as *"a J-cut (audio leads the picture) or L-cut (audio trails it)"* — **the model names his technique by name and has never executed it (H4).** **And a caution that matters before anyone builds against it: `qa/verify_edges.py` becomes the WRONG instrument here.** It certifies that no word is clipped at an edge, and **a mid-sentence splice deliberately cuts inside a sentence** — so a tool built to flag that will flag his own technique as a defect. **Third false-positive family after S44 and S47 — though it shrinks with the correction: splices are occasional, so this is a future false positive rather than a blocker on building anything now.** **SIZED 2026-09-12, and it came back the cheap way — asked rather than assumed.** *"I usually splice between words. I don't think I splice inside words to make the word itself shorter."* **So splice points land on word boundaries, the transcript already carries per-word start and end times, and this needs NO sub-word audio alignment.** A contained build rather than a research problem — which is the difference between scheduling it and deferring it indefinitely, decided by one question | open — **Medium, behind E5. Real but occasional, and explicitly not urgent.** **Exit: one spoken line renders with its picture taken from two different takes, joined so the splice is inaudible, and the review screen shows it as ONE line** **ANSWERED 2026-09-12, so the next person does not re-derive it: E5's `retakes` field does NOT carry E8, but it does not block it and it hands E8 its corpus.** The two want the same SHAPE — a list of `{start, end}` spans on one beat — with opposite semantics. `retakes` is a list of ALTERNATIVES, of which exactly one is live (the beat's own `start`/`end`); E8 needs a list of SEGMENTS that are all played in order. Overloading one field for both would make "what is on screen for this line" ambiguous, which is precisely the invariant at `types.ts:16-19` that E8 must preserve. So E8 wants a sibling field, not a migration of this one — and **no migration is needed, because `retakes` does not affect row count**: it is metadata on a single beat, so one line stays one row either way. **The useful part: E5 now PRESERVES every attempt instead of discarding it, so the fragments E8 composes from are on disk in `beats.json` rather than needing the transcript re-read.** E8 also inherits E5's widened picker region (`candidates/route.ts` searches the whole run), which is the same span a composition would draw from. | yes, not yet written — hand-author one composed beat in `beats.json` and render it; that is verifiable without any drafter changes |
| E9 | the speech floor on a joined project — mechanism unconfirmed, see below | **A joined project uses one speech floor for all its clips, and the third clip's words get cut in half.** `verify_removals.py` on the joined project: **`4 of 37 removals carry speech. The build is cutting words in half.`** Project speech averages **−26.7 dB**, floor **−34.7 dB**, and the four sit at −32.4, −33.5, −28.5 and −34.4 dB with **peaks to −10.9**. **All four are in IMG_0062's portion, after t=915.28s.** And the level rises **monotonically by clip**: clip 1 mean **−55.8 dB** (0 speech-carrying), clip 2 **−51.0** (0), clip 3 **−41.1** (**4**), with clip-3 peaks averaging **−21.9** against the project's own speech average of −26.7. **THE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES IT STICK:** `img-0063` — a single clip from the same batch, same build, same minute — reports **`All 13 removals are below the speech floor. Nothing audible was cut out of a line.`** with its own floor of −31.9 dB. `verify_edges` agrees directionally: **2 of 23** edge fragments carry speech on the joined project, **0 of 9** on the control. **MECHANISM IS A HYPOTHESIS, NOT A FINDING, and the row says so because she said so:** the single project-wide floor is **inferred from the tool's own output line, not read in the code.** **Desmond verifies the mechanism before anyone builds** — that is his lane and it is the discipline that paid on the nightly. **Suggested direction, hers: measure the floor over each source clip's own span rather than over the joined file**, which also turns `verify_removals` back into a usable gate for joined projects. **Why it is first in M0.85 rather than a jump:** *"the app quietly clipping words inside a cut it reports as finished, specific to the feature this milestone exists to deliver, with a clean control proving the join causes it"* — and **M0.85's gate is his ear**, so a cut that clips words cannot be put in front of him for a tightness judgement at all. Same prerequisite shape as S26 | **NOT A DEFECT — RETRACTED 2026-09-12 before a line was written, and the retraction is the row now. No word was clipped; the defect is in the tool.** **The one-step-before-Theo ruling is what bought this**, and it is the first of tonight's four wrong-cause findings caught by the role whose job it is rather than by the implementer mid-fix. **Three things settle it, all read rather than measured.** **(1) The verifier's floor is more sensitive than the detector whose decisions it audits.** `verify_removals.py:171-179` computes `floor = project_speech − 8` = **−34.7 dB** here, while the build's removal decision comes from `silence_map.py:14`'s **hardcoded −28 dB**, invoked at `lib/pipeline.ts:273` **with no `--noise` argument** — so the floor sits **6.7 dB below the threshold the build was entitled to trust**, and `(−34.7, −28]` is a **guaranteed false-positive band.** **(2) All four flagged spans are strictly inside regions ffmpeg itself certified below −28 dBFS**, reconstructed against the project's own `silence.txt` and matching `walk_pieces:232` **to the millisecond with 35 ms of margin at each end** — e.g. silence `1057.345979–1057.643104` → removal `1057.381–1057.608`. And **`beats.json` carries `holes: []` on every beat**, so none is an editor-drawn hole. **The build did exactly what it promises.** **(3) The tell: her four means are −28.5, −32.4, −33.5, −34.4 — all pressed against the −28 ceiling and not one above it.** That is a threshold disagreement, not speech. **And the control corroborates rather than refutes:** `img-0063` speech −23.9 → floor −31.9 → a band only 3.9 dB wide, with removals at −53 to −62, nowhere near it. **Her clip-3 clustering and monotonic rise are fully explained without the floor being per-clip-wrong** — which is why she was right to label the mechanism an inference and wrong only about which component was at fault. **Replaced by S47 (one of her four is S47's own span and number verbatim), S71, S72 and S73** | n/a — retracted |

**The pattern: four of the six are fields that would have explained something
the user is otherwise left guessing about, and two of them are the reason a
High-severity row cannot be seen from any screen.** Not bloat — **written and
not wired**, which is this codebase's signature defect and the third instance of
it tonight.

U10 is still accurate | open — blocked |
