Two real ones. **(a) False copy, user-facing:** Settings says the Associates tag is *"Appended to any Amazon link SnipAi puts in a caption"* — **SnipAi produces no captions anywhere; there is no caption surface in `app/` at all.** Same family as C32's toast, P20's log path and N13's guess: **copy that describes what the author intended rather than what the app does.** (b) The products list is **un-addable** — PATCH 404s on an unknown id and there is no POST | open |
| H8 | Voice input — **split 2026-09-11; this is the half that is deliverable today.** The learning-rejection dialog at `review/page.tsx:2523-2540` exists and has no mic; `<VoiceInput onText={setLearnText} />` at `:2534` is a ten-line change. The other place NOTES.md describes is the Level-3 free-text box, which does not exist yet — that is **H11**, and it is dependent on H1, not parallel to it. Splitting so the reachable half is not blocked behind the unreachable one | open |
| H10 | **Learning has no surface at all, and the surface he can see feeds nothing.** `learnFromEdits()` POSTs `--apply` from six edit paths and calls `setLearned(...)`, but the card that displays it is gated on `learnedOpen` and **`setLearnedOpen(true)` does not exist anywhere in the codebase**. Its own comment says the card "stays available in Settings" — it is not. **Settings reads `learnings.json` (prose preferences); learning writes `tuning.json` (numeric params). Two different stores**, and nothing in the app ever displays `tuning.json`, while grep finds no reader of `learnings.json` anywhere in `ugc-edit-system/`. So the toggles he can flip affect no cut, and the numbers that change every cut have no surface. **Two dead ends pointed at each other** — split out of H5, which read as one missing panel | open |
| H11 | Voice input in the **Level-3 free-text box** — split from H8 2026-09-11. Not deliverable in parallel: the box does not exist, so this is **dependent on H1** and cannot start before it | open — blocked on H1 |

**H12 — the definition cards contain unsourced claims, and the file says so
itself. Filed 2026-09-12 from Kayer's request: *"I want the app to look up the
actual definition of the ingredient."*** **This is the third thing tonight that
turned out already three-quarters built.**

**What exists, verified on disk rather than recalled.**
`~/Movies/SnipAi/reference/glossary.json` — **3,494 bytes, dated Sep 7, 15
terms** (Bakuchiol, Barrier, Ceramides, Collagen, Copper peptides, EGF, Fine
lines, Glutathione, Hyaluronic Acid, Hyaluronic acid, NAD+, Niacinamide,
Peptides, Retinol, Squalane). `plan_graphics.py:137` reads a `verified` flag per
term, `:170` carries it into the plan, `:195` prints
`[definition UNVERIFIED]`. **And the app-side gate is real, not asserted:**
`GraphicsPanel.tsx:112` takes `allowUnverified`, `:124-125` names the unverified
terms, `:134` lists unchecked definition cards, `:211` and `:268` are the
approve controls, and `graphics/route.ts:97-99` validates the boolean.

**So the mechanism he is asking for is BUILT, and the glossary's own `_note`
states the safety claim and it holds:** *"every definition here was drafted by
the assistant and none has been checked against a source... each carries
verified:false until Kayer confirms the wording. The app will not burn an
unverified card in without an explicit override."* **Checked: 0 of 15 are
verified, and the override path exists.** So **nothing unsourced has shipped
into a video**, and this row is deliberately not filed as a live harm.

**What is actually missing is the content and one piece of wiring.** (1) **All
15 definitions are assistant-drafted and none carries a source.** Sourcing 15
terms is bounded work — an afternoon of citation, not a network feature. (2)
**The glossary is not keyed to what he sells.** It is a skin/beauty set;
tonight's videos were **creatine** and glutathione, and **creatine is not in
it** — while R2 records that the products form *"writes a file only an
uninvoked tool reads."* Two unfed files that should feed each other. (3)
**`Hyaluronic Acid` and `Hyaluronic acid` are both present** — a case-duplicate
that will either produce two cards or match ambiguously.

**The pattern worth naming, because it is now three for three tonight:**
`reference/inspiration/` (house style from his own videos — **empty**), **H4**
(the J-cut — complete and **never invoked**), and this (**present and
unsourced**). **This app's problem is not missing features. It is features built
to ninety percent and then never fed or never invoked** — which is the fade
handle, the observation this docket was created for, recurring three times in
one night on three different features.

**Open — Medium, and not urgent: the gate holds.** **Exit: every term carries a
source he can see, the glossary covers the ingredients in his own products, and
the case-duplicate is gone.** See DOCKET R11 and B9 — **the one decision that is
his is whether offline curated definitions are enough, or whether he wants
arbitrary lookup, which needs a network path this app does not have.**

## Bloat — decisions, not defects

Do not act on these without saying so first.

| ID | What | The argument | Status |
|----|------|--------------|--------|
| B1 | The timeline NLE (~a third of the client) | NOTES.md Level 2 asks for "mark roughly where it's wrong, no detailed diagnosis". CLAUDE.md §5 argues pads should be computed off word gaps, not eyeballed. Keep span-delete and the `[`/`]` nudge; question reorder, fades, detached audio, five zoom levels | **wontfix 2026-09-11 — kept by decision.** Kayer asked for the full app with all of his features; the timeline stays. Not an oversight — a decision, and not to be re-litigated. |
| B2 | The graphics subsystem (~1,300 lines) | `CLAUDE.md:30` — "No captions, no graphic overlays, no PIP" — is the measured style target. Either the feature or that line is wrong; both are live | **wontfix 2026-09-11 — kept by decision.** Superseded by his call on `CLAUDE.md:30`: the graphics subsystem stays and the house-style rule was the thing that was wrong. See the E3 row — the rule must be rewritten so the scorecard stops penalising a feature he deliberately built and wants. |
| B3 | *(mostly pipeline)* Nine `.command` scripts, three broken | Collapse to `setup.command` + `launch-snipai.command` | open |
| B4 | *(pipeline)* The drop-folder watcher | Even repaired, it is a second ingest path that bypasses review — the workflow SnipAi exists to replace | **wontfix 2026-09-11 — delete it. Decided by the PM, because Kayer delegated this one specifically** (*"let the PM decide"*), not assumed. See the reasoning below the table |

**ANSWERED 2026-09-11 — do not loosen the clamp, fix the layout; built as
M3a.** A compact "line being spoken" strip under the player, so the picture
and the current line coexist without the full list needing to fit. The
question and its reasoning are kept below so the decision is not
re-litigated. Taken rather than held because Kayer had already delegated it
twice.

**Superseded question — the follow clamp (C28/C29b), re-asked because the
measured cost was not the cost he ruled on.** He was told "the player never
leaves the viewport, at a cost of following stopping after about three lines"
and chose that. The measured cost is **zero follow steps after line 1**, and
the consequence nobody had in front of them is that **at his 900pt display the
lines list is below the fold for the entire cut** — he can see the player or
the lines, never both, on the machine he actually works on. That is not the
tradeoff he agreed to.

**My recommendation: do not loosen the clamp — the clamp is not the problem.**
Loosening it buys back line-following by letting the player drift off screen,
which is the bug he reported twice (R4) and the one C22/C28 just closed. The
real problem is that a ~400pt player, a timeline band and a 34-row lines list
do not fit in 800pt of viewport, and the follow rule is being asked to solve a
space problem it cannot solve. **The fix is layout, not arithmetic.** Two
shapes worth putting to him: shrink or collapse the player during playback so
both fit, or keep the full list below and put a compact "line being spoken"
strip directly under the player, which is what following was for in the first
place. Either leaves C28's bound intact and makes it irrelevant.

**The one thing I would not do is nothing**, because the current state reads
as working — the player never leaves, no test fails, and the review flow is
silently unusable at his own screen size during playback.

---

**DECIDED 2026-09-11: delete the drop-folder ingest.** Kayer handed this one
back — *"let the PM decide"* — so it is a delegated call, not an assumed one.
The question and both arguments are kept below, unedited, because a decision
whose reasoning is not findable gets re-litigated.

**Why delete, and why the answer is firmer than it was this morning.** The
decisive fact changed today: **M0 closed.** Drop-in auto-cut through the
dashboard is proven, both routes — the `Import footage` picker and a real
Finder drag — verified in the packaged window with finished cuts out the far
end. The drop-folder's entire value proposition was *"footage appears, cut
appears"*, and that is now delivered by the path that also has a review flow
attached. The dashboard drag **is** a watched folder, with the part SnipAi
exists for bolted on. The repair argument was strong while drop-in ingest was
unproven; it is not strong against a working one.

Against that: repairing it means fixing four dead tool references, a `flock`
guard that cannot work on macOS, and a loop that never moves anything out of
`inbox/` — on a path nobody has successfully used in some time. **And on a
one-person project, every live path is a path that can break and cost a
session.** Tonight is the argument: four separate doors onto "the port is our
server" (N1, P19, T10, N8) each cost real time, and they cost it *because*
there were four. Deleting a broken second door is worth more here than the
convenience it never actually delivered.

**Kayer's "this app is just for me right now" does not rescue it.** That
statement weakens the surface-area argument — with one user there is no
confusion cost in having two ways in — but the surface-area argument was
always the weakest of the three. Broken-and-unused and maintenance-per-path
both survive it intact, and the second gets *stronger* when one person
maintains everything.

**What it does to B3's nine `.command` scripts.** Two of the nine go with it:
`process_inbox.sh` and `build-v7.command`, which are also two of the three
broken ones. That **moots P7 entirely** (the `flock` guard nobody can reach)
and closes **most of P4** — its live entry points into `tools/_superseded/`
are in exactly these files, leaving only the two `CLAUDE.md` references, which
are documentation and a one-line edit. **B3 itself is not decided by this** —
Kayer delegated the drop-folder question, not the whole collapse — so B3 stays
open on the remaining seven, now a smaller and duller question than it was.

**Execution is a dev row, not a PM edit.** Nothing here is deleted by me.

---

**The original question, kept for the record — B3 + B4, the drop-folder
ingest. Delete it, or repair it?** Raised 2026-09-11; **not covered by the
standing order**, which
names B1, B2, B5 and B6 as kept-by-decision and is silent on these two, so
this is genuinely undecided rather than re-litigation.

What is true today: the drop-folder path is broken end to end, not
degraded. `process_inbox.sh:32` calls `tools/auto_edit.py`, `build-v7.command:5`
calls `tools/tune_and_build.py`, and `CLAUDE.md:60`/`:97` name two more — **all
four exist only in `tools/_superseded/`** (P4). On top of that, P7's `flock`
guard returns 0 down both branches because macOS has no `flock`, and the loop
never `mv`s or `rm`s anything out of `inbox/`, so every tick reprocesses every
file forever. Nothing here has worked for some time and nothing noticed.

**Recommendation at the time: delete it.** (Adopted 2026-09-11.) Repairing P7's lock inside a script whose only
tool call is a dead path is work spent on a second way into the app that
skips the review flow — and the review flow is what SnipAi *is*. He already
has drop-in ingest through the dashboard, which is M0 and which he uses; this
is the pre-SnipAi workflow surviving underneath it. Deleting it also closes
most of B3's nine `.command` scripts for free.

**The counter-argument, so he is choosing and not just agreeing:** a watched
folder is genuinely less friction than opening the app, and if what he wants
is "footage appears, cut appears, I review later", that is a real workflow and
not a bug. If he says that, the answer is not to repair `process_inbox.sh` but
to point a watcher at the dashboard's own import path, so there is still one
ingest and one review flow.
| B5 | Four independent undo mechanisms | 60 snapshots + 5-day trash + in-page history + the delete toast, for one user editing one JSON file | **wontfix 2026-09-11 — kept by decision.** All four undo mechanisms stay. He asked for everything. |
| B6 | Five settings tabs | Products feeds nothing; Profile is a hardcoded nameplate | **wontfix 2026-09-11 — kept by decision.** All five settings tabs stay. He asked for everything. |

# Out of scope — the `ugc-edit-system` pipeline

Kept because they are real and were verified, not because they are queued. Run
`./scripts/qa --with-pipeline` to check them, and say so explicitly if you want
them fixed.

## Pipeline

**Audited 2026-09-11. Only P6 is on a shipped, wanted feature.** QA grepped
`lib/` and `app/` for every tool name in this section; `motion_graphics.py` is
the only one that comes back (`lib/pipeline.ts:315`, the live "Render graphics"
job). P9 (`add_sfx`), P14 (`calibrate_silence`), P18 (`align_reference`) and
P3's survivor (`find_overlay_cues`) are all in tools the app never invokes —
they are U1/U2's territory, and fixing them buys nothing a user can see.
**P6 is scheduled; the rest wait for M4**, when the keep/cut answers land.

**Dispatch groups** (named `PG` so they do not collide with repo hygiene's
`G1-G7`):

| # | The one rule | Rows |
|---|--------------|------|
| PG1 | **Clamped at write *and* at read.** P2 now clamps on the way in; P1's read side has no bound at all, so at P2's own 0.35 ceiling the out-point still walks outward into the next take, ×34 beats. Fix one and you have fixed neither. Pull `snap()` out of `main()` while in there — it is the stated blocker on both tests | P1, P2 |
| PG2 | **A fresh install resolves every path.** All three are `snipai-release`'s, not the pipeline's, and all three are invisible on this Mac because this Mac already has the artifacts | P13's survivor, N2, P16 |
| PG3 | **One parser, three copies.** Both have red tests already, and the corrected implementation exists in one of the files | P5, P10 |
| PG4 | **The drop-folder ingest.** Not a fix — a question for Kayer. See the note under Bloat | P4, P7, B3, B4 |
| PG5 | **ffmpeg defaults that silently do nothing.** Do P6 only; P9's tool is uninvoked | P6, P9 |

**Test-provability, because it changes who proves them.** Automatable today:
P1, P2, P3, P4, P8, P13, P14, P15, P17, P18. Already automated and red: P5,
P10. **Needs a real render and would pass a string assertion while still
broken: P6 and P9** — that is the `verify_edges.py` trap a second time, so
treat a green string test on either as no evidence. Not automatable at all:
P11.

**Leave this alone:** P5 and P10's regression tests are invisible to
`scripts/test` because `unittest discover` cannot import `tests/regressions`
(no `__init__.py`), and `scripts/qa --regressions` runs them file-by-file with
a comment saying exactly why. That is deliberate and correct — the bug board
is not the suite.

| ID | Where | Defect | Status | Test |
|----|-------|--------|--------|------|
| P1 | `build_cut.py:386`, `:389` | Out-point snapping has no clamp back to the original end, so a large `snap_tail` extends beats instead of tightening them. Lines corrected (`:227` was stale). **Read this with P2** — P2 now clamps on the way *in*, but the read side here has no bound at all, so even at P2's own 0.35 ceiling the out-point still moves outward into the next take, ×34 beats. Fix one and you have fixed neither | open | not yet — `snap()` is a closure inside `main()`; extracting it is part of the fix |
| P2 | `learn_from_edits.py:364`, `:375` | **Partial.** Learned parameters floored at 0 and never capped. Commit `9752e80` added the `LIMITS` ceiling, with the 1.359s incident quoted in its comment — `snap_tail` can no longer be poisoned, which was the original failure. Still uncapped at `:375`: `tuning["scoring"][...] = round(max(0.0, cur + delta), 3)`, no `LIMITS` entry. The surviving failure is slower and quieter than the original: repeatedly pick a take carrying an `internal_repeat` flag, `penalty_internal_repeat` climbs past 1.0 after ~10 runs, nothing clears `min_confidence: 0.7`, and **the take picker silently stops recommending anything** for any beat with a repeat flag. Nothing says it has stopped | open — partial | not yet — the clamp is inline in `main()`; extracting it is part of the fix |

The survivor is line-accurate but **dead code**: `plan_graphics.py:28` sets its own `DEFAULT_GLOSSARY` via `data_path`, and the stale constant is reachable only through a CLI that is already U2 "unused". Fix it for free if you are in the file, or delete the file; do not spend a slot | open — dead code | no, and not worth one |

The module docstring at `:14-16` asserts the opposite, which is how it survived this long. **The one pipeline row on a shipped, wanted feature** — `lib/pipeline.ts:315` invokes `motion_graphics.py` as the live "Render graphics" job, and the graphics subsystem is kept by Kayer's standing order. **Scheduled — see DOCKET M4-adjacent note; the rest of the pipeline waits** | open | **not a string assertion.** Asserting `eval=frame` is in the filter proves the flag is present, not that the geometry moves — the `verify_edges.py` trap again.
